Temporal spike: whether a Linux delegated cgroup ends an agent's whole tree when its user has no login session
<!-- ABOUTME: Spike card: whether an unprivileged Linux supervisor gets a delegated cgroup, and ends an agent's whole tree with it, when its user has no login session. -->

Part of the **Temporal** track, epic #1454 (whole-tree containment). **Author:** the PM.

**The track.** The Temporal track is a post-v0 exploration; its record is #1453. Three spikes (#1429, #1430, #1431) measured what Temporal gives a card's dispatch and what it does not. #1453 recommends going ahead after v0 on condition, in this order: whole-tree containment on every OS (E1), a design that stops a second attempt running alongside the first (E2), and only then the Temporal runtime (E3) and the board as a mirror (E4). The owner ruled the four epics on 2026-11-21 ([1453#7072953923](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1453#issuecomment-7072953923)). Cards are filed just in time; this one is in its epic's first wave. Nothing here records a decision, amends a requirement or changes `ARCHITECTURE.md` or `[name withheld].md`; a finding that calls for one is a proposal for the PM, the architect and the owner.

---

**The question.** On Linux, can an unprivileged supervisor obtain a delegated cgroup, place a dispatch's tree in it, and end the whole tree with `cgroup.kill`, when the supervisor's user has no open login session: when it starts with none open, and when the user's last session closes while the tree runs?

**Why a spike.** #1430 measured the delegated cgroup only while a login session was open, with `[name withheld]` running. Its report says the next card must not assume the systemd user manager exists, and names a host without it as something that would reverse its Linux recommendation (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment.md`, "Linux under WSL2: a delegated cgroup per dispatch, ended by cgroup.kill"). An engine runs unattended, so the user it runs as will often have no session. Whether a user manager, and so a delegated subtree, exists then, and what happens to a running tree when the last session closes, depends on systemd's and logind's behaviour on the host.

**What exists already, as context that earns no credit.**

- #1430's fixture, supervisor and runners: `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/` and `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/runners/`, and its members M1 to M6 ("The fixture"). The spike may reuse them, and says if it changed them.
- #1430's w1, w2 and w5 results with a session open: `cgroup.kill` ended all six members, and a fresh writer ended the orphaned cgroup after the supervisor was killed ("The table").
- #1430's open question names one route: rerun w1 with every session closed and `loginctl enable-linger` unset, and [name withheld] that enabling linger is a configuration change ("Questions this leaves open").

**The setup.**

- **Host:** Ubuntu on the WSL2 machine, the only Linux host the track has.
- **The routes it weighs,** at least these three:
  - **R0:** no session open and linger unset, nothing else changed;
  - **RL:** `loginctl enable-linger` set for the supervisor's user;
  - **RS:** a system-level unit that runs the supervisor as the unprivileged user with a delegated cgroup.
  The spike may weigh more and says why. RL and RS are host-configuration changes, named in the stated-intent comment before they are made and reverted afterwards.
- **The cases,** each run under every route that yields what it needs:
  - **N1:** under each route that yields a writable delegated cgroup with no session open, the supervisor starts while the user has no login session open, then ends the tree with `cgroup.kill` (#1430's E1);
  - **N2:** under each route that yields a writable delegated cgroup with a session open, R0 included, the supervisor starts while a session is open, and every session of the user is closed while the tree runs. The tree is then ended with `cgroup.kill`. R0 under N2 is the engine's own case: a user who logs out, with linger unset, while a tree runs. Closing every session is a step the "Siblings share hosts" bullet below governs;
  - **N3:** under each route that yields a writable delegated cgroup with no session open, the supervisor is killed outright with `kill -9` while no session is open, and a fresh process writes `cgroup.kill` to the orphaned cgroup (#1430's E2 with the fresh writer).
- **No live process** means `cgroup.events` reads `populated 0`, or the cgroup is gone, as #1430 read it ("Linux under WSL2: a delegated cgroup per dispatch, ended by cgroup.kill").
- **If no route yields a writable cgroup with no session open,** the card is still done: N1 and N3 are answered "not obtained" under every route, with each route's command and full output, and the recommendation says so.
- **Survival** is read at least 10 s after an ending, the bound #1430 used.
- **How the spike reaches WSL with no session open** is its own choice, and it says how its own access affected the user's sessions. A command it runs to observe must not open the session the case requires to be absent; where it cannot avoid one, it says so and the case counts as not run.

**Root.** Root to install or revert a route's configuration, such as RS's system unit, is inside this card and is a host-configuration change. A supervisor that itself runs as root is outside it.

**Scope edges.** Linux outside WSL2, a supervisor that runs as root, a member moved out of its cgroup on purpose, and the causes of WSL shutting down while idle are outside this card; whether WSL kept running during a case is inside it. The report names what WSL2 leaves unproved about a native Linux host.

**Hosts, and whose custody each is under.** #1429's section "Hosts, and whose custody each is under" binds this card as written there, except for the Mac mini's rule, which is below: the WSL2 machine and the Windows machine as the primary hosts and how each is reached, and the laptop's list of what it never takes. #1430's report found that `wsl-hostpc` did not answer and reached WSL through `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e …` (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment.md`, "What ran, and where"). Any use of the Mac mini goes through the rigger-d1 coordinator first: the spike asks rigger-ae on this card, waits for rigger-ae to relay rigger-d1's OK, and runs every command there through the mini's suite-slot script (`tools/coordinator/suite-slot.sh` in this repository), at the path and with the environment rigger-d1's terms give at dispatch, so that it takes the same slot M8's work does.

**Rules on how it runs.** #1429's "Rules on how it runs" binds this card as written there, with this card's number in place of #1429's, except its "Sibling spikes share the WSL2 machine" rule, which the "Siblings share hosts" bullet below replaces. Three of #1429's rules cost the last three spikes several judge rounds, so this card tightens them:

- **Exact commands.** The transcript holds every command exactly as it ran, character for character, with its UTC time and host. A command written up from [name withheld], or condensed, is not a transcript entry. A [name withheld] figure names the transcript entries of the commands that produced both its endpoints.
- **Failed runs in full.** A run that failed, was abandoned or was repeated keeps its full, unabridged output under `docs/spikes/<report>/`. The report says why each one failed or was abandoned.
- **Every pid, recorded at its start.** The processes that count are the supervisor, every fixture member, and every other process still running when the command that started it returned, on any host and under any account. A process that a spike process spawns may be recorded by the process that spawns it, at the moment it spawns it. A command that exits before it returns is covered by its transcript entry, and needs no record entry.
  - Each entry in the process record, under `docs/spikes/<report>/`, carries the process's host, its pid, its creation time as the host reports it, and the UTC time the entry was written. The entry is written as the process starts and before the next command on that host.
  - A process is the recorded one only where both its pid and its creation time match an entry; a pid alone is not a match, because Windows reuses pids quickly.
  - A pid found afterwards in a listing is a disclosure, not a record. The spike ends only recorded processes and their descendants (`AGENTS.md`, "End only recorded processes").
- **Siblings share hosts.** The sibling set is #1458, #1459, #1460, #1462 and #1463, and any of them may touch the WSL2 machine or the Windows machine while this card runs. Before a WSL restart, a `.wslconfig` or `/etc/wsl.conf` change, a logout of a user's sessions, or any other step that ends processes it did not start, the spike posts the step on each other card in that set and waits for each one's spike, or rigger-ae, to answer that none of its recorded processes would be ended.

---COMMENT by williacj at 2026-11-22T02:48:02Z---
**Pre-dispatch attack** (architect, under `.claude/skills/acceptance/` "Have it attacked before dispatch": items depend on a system outside Rigger's code). Every citation was opened on `origin/main` at 280ec1bf. The card asks for exact [name withheld] commands, full output for failed runs, and every pid recorded at its start. Its items 17–24 under "Acceptance" make each of those [name withheld]. The findings follow.

1. **It is unclear whether N2 runs under R0, and that is the main cell.** The cases run "for each route that yields a writable delegated cgroup". With no session open, R0 yields none. With a session open, it yields one (#1430, w1). So a maker can read R0 as yielding nothing and skip N2 under R0. Yet the engine's real case is a user who logs out, with linger unset, while a tree runs. **Fix:** name the cells: N1 and N3 under each route that yields a cgroup with no session open; N2 under each route that yields one with a session open, R0 included.
2. **Something other than `cgroup.kill` can end the tree.** A member may die before the write because the user manager stopped on the last logout, WSL stopped while idle (#1430 [name withheld] WSL stops when idle, "Recommendations", Linux), or systemd's `KillMode` sent `SIGTERM`, which M5 survives but others do not (w6). Item 8 reads survival only 10 s after the ending, so it credits `cgroup.kill` with those deaths. **Fix:** for every run, add a listing taken immediately before the ending that shows each member alive. Report a run where any member had died before the ending as not measuring `cgroup.kill`, and say what ended it.
3. **N3 has no timing item.** Item 9 times N1 and N2 only. N3, the fresh writer, is the analogue of `R-STATE-10`'s next-start reap, which E1 will build on. **Fix:** extend item 9 to N3.
4. **"The cgroup reporting no live process" names no reading.** **Fix:** name it as #1430 did: `cgroup.events` reads `populated 0`, or the cgroup is gone ("Recommendations", Linux).
5. **Root can be read two ways.** The scope edges put out of scope "a route needing root for the supervisor itself", while RS installs a system unit, which needs root. **Fix:** say that root to install or revert a route's configuration is in scope, and that a supervisor running as root is out of scope.
6. **Two sibling rules bind at once.** "Rules on how it runs" binds #1429's rules "as written there, with this card's number in place of #1429's". That carries in #1429's "Sibling spikes share the WSL2 machine", which says to post on "all three cards", now the closed #1429–#1431. This card's own "Siblings share hosts" bullet says to post on "each sibling card" and names only "the E1 spikes". A maker cannot tell which set to wait for. **Fix:** say this card's bullet replaces #1429's rule, and name the sibling set as #1458, #1459, #1460, #1462 and #1463. All five may touch the WSL2 or Windows machine. This card's N2 is itself such a step: closing every session of the WSL user is the "logout of a user's sessions" that bullet names.
7. **"Every process it starts" has no stated scope.** It is unclear whether it covers the fixture members the fixture spawns, the supervisor, one-shot commands such as a listing or `ssh`, and processes run under another account. Each reading gives a different process record, and so a different ruling on "Each entry in the process record carries a recorded time earlier than…". **Fix:** define it as every process still running when the command that started it returned, plus the supervisor and every fixture member. A process that a spike process spawns may be recorded by the process that spawns it, at the moment it spawns it. A command that exits before returning is covered by its transcript entry.

ABOUTME: Spike findings for card #1430: whether cgroup v2 under WSL2 and Job Objects on Windows end an
agent's whole process tree, measured against the macOS [name withheld] and today's route.

# Whole-tree containment on Linux under WSL2 and on Windows

## The answer

On Linux under WSL2, the answer is yes. An unprivileged supervisor obtained a cgroup it could write
to from the systemd user manager, with no root. Writing cgroup.kill ended all six fixture
members, including the two that left the session and the one that moved to `/`. The cgroup reported
no live process between 0.88 and 1.66 ms after the write, in the three runs that measured it. When the supervisor was
killed outright, all six members lived on in the orphaned cgroup. A fresh process that wrote
cgroup.kill to that cgroup ended every one of them.

On native Windows, the answer is also yes, with one condition. A Node supervisor reached Job Objects
through [name withheld], an [name withheld] package with a [name withheld] binary. Every member it started, M1 to M4 and M6, was
in its job. `[name withheld]` ended all of them. With kill-on-job-close set, killing the
supervisor with `[name withheld] /F` ended every member too. Without kill-on-job-close, every member
outlived the supervisor. A fresh process could not then reach the job, because its name disappeared
with the supervisor's last handle. The condition is that this supervisor ran elevated (High
Mandatory Level). A non-elevated supervisor was not measured.

Today's route (`R-STATE-17`) left M6 alive on both macOS and Linux. M6 is the member that starts a
new session and changes its working directory to `/`. The group kill and the working-directory
census found every other member. On macOS, the process-group kill alone also left M3 alive, the
member that starts a new session. Killing the supervisor outright left every member alive on macOS,
as `R-STATE-9` and `R-STATE-10` expect.

Round 2 repeated every measurement on every host with every process the runs started recorded by
pid, as the judge's round-1 verdict asked. Each repeat gave the same result for every member
("Round 2", below).

I recommend a delegated cgroup ended by cgroup.kill for Linux under WSL2, and a Job Object with
kill-on-job-close for native Windows. Both are under "Recommendations", with what would reverse
each. This report hands over evidence. It changes no code, requirement or recorded decision.

## What ran, and where

| Run | Host | Ending | Route the supervisor was started by |
|---|---|---|---|
| m1 | laptop (macOS) | E1, process-group kill | `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/runners/mac-run.sh`, run locally on the laptop |
| m2 | laptop | [name withheld], today's route | the same |
| m3 | laptop | E2, `kill -9` of the supervisor | the same |
| w1, w7 | WSL2 | E1, cgroup.kill | `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e bash -s` ran `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/runners/wsl-run.sh` inside WSL. That started the supervisor inside WSL as a transient systemd user service (`systemd-run --user`) |
| w2 | WSL2 | [name withheld], today's route | the same |
| w5 | WSL2 | E2, `kill -9` of the supervisor, then a fresh cgroup.kill writer | the same |
| w3 | WSL2 | E2, a first attempt | the same. Its fresh-writer loop ran on after the cgroup was removed (under "What failed"). w5 replaced it |
| w6 | WSL2 | E2 with systemd's default `KillMode` (supplementary) | the same |
| x1, x2 | Windows | E1, `[name withheld]` | `ssh hostpc` ran `node win-run.cjs` in the SSH session. That started the supervisor detached |
| x3 | Windows | E2, `[name withheld] /F`, kill-on-job-close set | the same |
| x4 | Windows | E2, `[name withheld] /F`, kill-on-job-close not set, then a fresh process's reach | the same |
| mr1, mr2, mr3 | laptop | round 2: E1, [name withheld], E2 | `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/orch.cjs` ran `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/driver-posix.cjs` locally |
| wr1, wr2, wr3 | WSL2 | round 2: E1, [name withheld], E2 with the fresh writer | orch.cjs ran `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e bash -s fx1430r2` with a script on stdin. The script [name withheld] into the driver in a transient `systemd-run --user --scope` unit, and the driver starts each supervisor as a transient systemd user service, as round 1 did |
| xr1, xr2, xr3 | Windows | round 2: E1, E2 with kill-on-job-close, E2 without it and the fresh reach | orch.cjs ran `ssh hostpc` with `node driver-win.cjs`. That started each supervisor detached |

WSL was reached only through `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e …`, because `wsl-hostpc` did not answer. In every
WSL run, the process that launched the tree, the Node supervisor, lived inside WSL. It was a child
of the user's systemd manager (pid 339), as each listing's parent pid column shows. It was never a
Windows process.

`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/` holds the evidence:

- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/transcript.txt`, round 1's commands run through its wrapper.
- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/`, the fixture source for macOS and Linux.
- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/`, the fixture source for Windows.
- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/runners/`, the scripts that drove the round-1 runs.
- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/`, round 2's code, its transcript and its process records.
- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/session-log.txt`, the tool calls in the spike engineer's
  retained session record, with the gaps "The transcript" lists.
- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/ci-fast-136023691075-excerpt.txt`, the failure output of the
  CI run that failed on this pull request.

## The fixture

The fixture is one process tree started in a dispatch directory the spike made: `mac/d-<run>` in
the laptop's spike directory, `~/1430-spike-engineer/d-<run>` in WSL, and
`C:\Users\[name withheld]\1430-spike-engineer\d-<run>` on Windows. Each member sleeps 300 s unless ended. On
macOS and Linux, `argv[0]` is `fx-<run>-M<n>`. On Windows, the command line carries
`fx1430-<run>-M<n>`.

### How each member was started

| Member | macOS and Linux (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/fixture.sh`, run by `bash` as the supervised command) | Windows (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/fixture-win.cjs`, run by `node` as the supervised command) |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | `bash -c "(exec -a fx-R-M2 sleep 300) & exec -a fx-R-M1 sleep 300" &`: a direct child of the supervised command | `spawn(node, [member-win.cjs, …-M1, 'M1'])`: a direct child |
| M2 | the [name withheld] M1 forks before it [name withheld], a grandchild whose parent is M1 | M1 runs `spawn(node, [member-win.cjs, …-M2, 'leaf'])`, a grandchild whose parent is M1 |
| M3 | `perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid(); exec {"sleep"} "fx-R-M3", "300"' &`: a new session | `spawn(node, […-M3], { detached: true })`. [name withheld]'s src/win/process.c (read at `v1.51.0`) makes this `DETACHED_PROCESS \| CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`, a new process group |
| M4 | `bash -c "(exec -a fx-R-M4 sleep 300) & exit 0" &`: its parent exits at once, so it is reparented | `cmd.exe /d /c start "" /b node member-win.cjs …-M4 leaf`. Its parent cmd.exe exits at once |
| M5 | `bash -c "trap '' TERM; exec -a fx-R-M5 sleep 300" &`: `SIGTERM` is ignored, and `SIG_[name withheld]` survives `exec` | not applicable: `[name withheld]` cannot be ignored |
| M6 | `perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid(); chdir "/"; exec {"sleep"} "fx-R-M6", "300"' &`: a new session working in `/` | `spawn(node, […-M6, 'M6'], { detached: true })`. The member runs `process.chdir()` to the drive root, `C:\` |

The supervised command waits on stdin for one line before it starts any member. The supervisor moves
it into the cgroup or job first, and then writes the line. That closes the race between spawning
and assigning. Every supervisor spawned the supervised command with `detached: true`, as
`[name withheld]` in `src/substrate/process.mjs` does, so that command leads a process group of its own.

### The supervisors

- **macOS and Linux:** `fixture/supervisor.cjs <e1|[name withheld]|e2> <run> <dispatch> <fixture-dir>`.
  - On Linux, it reads its own cgroup from `/proc/self/cgroup` and creates a child cgroup `tree`
    under it. It writes the supervised command's pid into tree/cgroup.procs.
  - E1 on Linux writes `1` to tree/cgroup.kill. It then polls tree/cgroup.events every 1 ms
    until it reads `populated 0`.
  - E1 on macOS sends `SIGKILL` to the process group.
  - [name withheld], on both, sends `SIGKILL` to the process group. It then runs
    `[name withheld] -w -n -P -a -d cwd -u <uid> -F pun`, the argument list `listing` in
    `src/substrate/process.mjs` builds, and sends `SIGKILL` to every pid whose working directory
    lies under the dispatch directory's real path.
  - It lists 3 s after the start and 12 s after the ending.
- **Windows:** `fixture-win/supervisor-win.cjs <e1|e2k|e2n> fx1430-<run>-sup <dispatch>`.
  - It creates a job named `Local\fx1430-<run>` with `[name withheld]`. For e1 and e2k, it sets
    `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` with `[name withheld]([name withheld])`.
    For e2n it leaves the limit unset.
  - It assigns the supervised command with `[name withheld]`.
  - E1 calls `[name withheld]`. It then polls `[name withheld]`
    until the count is 0.
  - It lists 5 s after the start and 12 s after the ending.
- **The listings:**
  - `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/list.sh` uses `ps` for pid, parent pid, group and state, and os.[name withheld] for the
    session. It takes the working directory from `/proc/<pid>/cwd` on Linux and from `[name withheld] -d cwd`
    on macOS, and the cgroup from `/proc/<pid>/cgroup` on Linux.
  - `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/win32.cjs` uses `Win32_Process` for pid, parent pid and session. It calls
    `[name withheld]` against the spike's job and against any job. It reads the working directory
    from the process's [name withheld] (`[name withheld]_USER_PROCESS_PARAMETERS.[name withheld]`).

## Linux under WSL2

### The user, cgroup v2, systemd and cgroup.kill

**The user (w7, transcript entry 01:01:51Z).** The unit's `ExecStart` ran `id`, and then `exec`ed into the Node
supervisor in the same process. It printed:

```
uid=1000([name withheld]) gid=1000([name withheld]) groups=1000([name withheld]),4([name withheld]),24([name withheld]),27([name withheld]),30(dip),46([name withheld]),100(users)
```

Runs w1 to w6 started the supervisor the same way, without the `id` prefix. Their runner printed the
same `id` line. The supervisor ran as the unprivileged user `[name withheld]`, not root. The user is in `[name withheld]`,
but nothing used it.

**cgroup v2 is mounted, and the unified hierarchy is in use (00:41:17Z).** `mount | grep cgroup`
printed one line, a `cgroup2` mount at `/sys/fs/cgroup` and no v1 hierarchy:

```
cgroup2 on /sys/fs/cgroup type cgroup2 (rw,[name withheld],[name withheld],[name withheld],[name withheld],[name withheld])
```

`cat /sys/fs/cgroup/cgroup.controllers` printed `[name withheld] io [name withheld] pids [name withheld]`. At
00:41:04Z, `stat -fc %T /sys/fs/cgroup` printed `cgroup2fs`.

**systemd runs as pid 1 (00:41:04Z).** `ps -p 1 -o pid,comm` printed:

```
    PID COMMAND
      1 systemd
```

At 00:41:17Z, `systemctl is-system-running` printed `running`, and `systemctl --user is-system-running`
also printed `running`. `loginctl show-user [name withheld]` showed `Service=[name withheld]` and `State=active`.

**cgroup.kill exists (00:41:17Z).** `ls /sys/fs/cgroup` listed cgroup.kill at the root.
`ls -l /sys/fs/cgroup/non-systemd` showed `--w------- 1 root root 0 Nov 19 18:41 cgroup.kill`. The
delegated cgroup in the next item holds a cgroup.kill owned by `[name withheld]`.

**An unprivileged user can obtain a writable cgroup subtree, through the systemd user manager
(00:42:55Z).** Two routes worked, and the runs used the second:

```
$ systemd-run --user --scope -p Delegate=yes cat /proc/self/cgroup
Running as unit: run-p511-i2417.scope; invocation ID: f03feebebecb4105b3116a22f05c16e8
0::/user.slice/[name withheld]/app.slice/run-p511-i2417.scope
$ systemd-run --user --wait --pipe -q -p Delegate=yes bash -c "cat /proc/self/cgroup; c=$(cut -d: -f3 /proc/self/cgroup); ls -l /sys/fs/cgroup$c | grep -E \"procs|kill|subtree\"; id"
0::/user.slice/[name withheld]/app.slice/run-p512-i2418.service
--w------- 1 [name withheld] 0 Nov 19 18:42 cgroup.kill
-rw-r--r-- 1 [name withheld] 0 Nov 19 18:42 cgroup.procs
-rw-r--r-- 1 [name withheld] 0 Nov 19 18:42 cgroup.subtree_control
uid=1000([name withheld]) gid=1000([name withheld]) groups=1000([name withheld]),4([name withheld]),24([name withheld]),27([name withheld]),30(dip),46([name withheld]),100(users)
```

The shell that `wsl.exe -e` starts is not in systemd's tree. Its `/proc/self/cgroup` read
`0::/non-systemd` at 00:41:04Z, a root-owned cgroup it cannot write to. The scope route moved even
that process into a unit of the user manager. The service route starts the process inside the
unit. Neither needed root, so the root route was not measured.

98	103729  339     103729  103729  Ss    /home/[name withheld]/1430-spike-engineer/d-w5 | [name withheld] | bash /home/[name withheld]/1430-spike-engineer/fixture/fixture.sh w5
99	103730  103729  103729  103729  S     /home/[name withheld]/1430-spike-engineer/d-w5 | [name withheld] | fx-w5-M1 300
100	103731  103729  103731  103731  Ss    /home/[name withheld]/1430-spike-engineer/d-w5 | [name withheld] | fx-w5-M3 300
101	103733  103729  103729  103729  S     /home/[name withheld]/1430-spike-engineer/d-w5 | [name withheld] | fx-w5-M5 300
102	103734  103729  103734  103734  Ss    / | [name withheld] | fx-w5-M6 300
103	103735  103730  103729  103729  S     /home/[name withheld]/1430-spike-engineer/d-w5 | [name withheld] | fx-w5-M2 300
104	103736  339     103729  103729  S     /home/[name withheld]/1430-spike-engineer/d-w5 | [name withheld] | fx-w5-M4 300
105	count=7
106	```
107	
108	M1 to M6 were all alive, still in the orphaned `tree` cgroup. systemd reported the unit as
109	`ActiveState=failed` with `Result=signal`.
110	
111	**The fresh writer.** A new `bash`, pid 103890, which was no part of the supervisor, wrote `1` to
112	`…/fx1430-w5.service/tree/cgroup.kill`. It found the path from the unit's `ControlGroup` property.
113	It reported `tree cgroup empty or removed (exists: no) after 6019 us`. Once the cgroup emptied,
114	systemd removed the failed unit's cgroup, and that is why the cgroup no longer existed. Ten seconds
115	later:
116	
117	```
118	listing run=w5 at 2026-11-20T00:52:36Z on Linux
119	PID     PPID    PGID    SID     STAT  CWD | CGROUP | ARGS
120	count=0
121	```
122	
123	Every remaining member had ended. The first attempt, w3, gave the same before and after readings.
124	Its listing at 00:51:55Z read `count=0`.
125	
126	### A supplementary reading: E2 under systemd's default `KillMode` (w6)
127	
128	This run is outside the card's endings, because systemd acts in it. Under the default
129	`KillMode=control-group`, systemd sent `SIGTERM` to the unit's cgroup when the supervisor died.
130	Twelve seconds after `kill -9 103945`:
131	
132	```
133	listing run=w6 at 2026-11-20T00:52:58Z on Linux
134	PID     PPID    PGID    SID     STAT  CWD | CGROUP | ARGS
135	103959  339     103955  103955  S     /home/[name withheld]/1430-spike-engineer/d-w6 | [name withheld] | fx-w6-M5 300
136	count=1
137	```
138	
139	Only M5, which ignores `SIGTERM`, was alive, and the unit was still in `stop-sigterm`. The fresh
140	cgroup.kill writer then ended it, and the listing 10 s later read `count=0`. A unit used as a
141	dispatch's container therefore needs `[name withheld]=SIGKILL`, or an explicit cgroup.kill, to close M5.
142	
143	## Round 2: every measurement repeated, with every process recorded
144	
145	The round-1 judge found that round 1 recorded only the long-lived processes. It never recorded the
146	pids of short-lived helpers, or of the intermediate process that M4's parent is on macOS and Linux
147	("Every process the spike started", below). Those pids cannot be recovered, so round 2 repeated
148	every card measurement, on every host, on 2026-11-20, with every process the runs started
149	recorded. The stated intent for the new steps went up first, at
150	[1430#7044905230](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1430#issuecomment-7044905230).
151	
152	### How each process was recorded
153	
154	| Host | What recorded it |
155	|---|---|
156	| All | Every Node process loads `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/rec.cjs`. It writes the process's own pid and parent, and the pid, command and exit of every process it starts, as JSON lines. The laptop's step runner, `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/orch.cjs`, records each `ssh` and local command the same way, and writes `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/transcript-r2.txt`: each step's exact argv, its stdin script verbatim with sha256, its UTC start and end, and its full output |
157	| macOS and Linux | `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/fixture.sh` writes the pid of every process it starts, M4's parent included, using bash builtins only. The listing (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/list.cjs`) runs in Node, with one recorded `ps`. On macOS it adds one recorded `[name withheld] -O` and one recorded `python3`, run by its real path rather than through the `/usr/bin/python3` stub |
158	| WSL | Each script fed to `bash -s` writes its own pid and its two WSL init parents, and runs every external command through a `rec` function that prints the command's pid, using bash builtins only. Each supervisor's unit prints the pid of its `id` and of its own bash, which then [name withheld] Node |
159	| Windows | A watcher, `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/watcher.ps1`, ran in its own SSH session from 16:34:25Z to 16:41:43Z. It logged every process start and stop on the machine through [name withheld]'s `Win32_[name withheld]` and `Win32_[name withheld]`. `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/collect-win.cjs` then picks out the spike's own processes: the watcher's session chain, every per-connection `sshd` that started within 3 s of one of the laptop's recorded `ssh` spawns, and every descendant of either. The result is `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/win-mine-full.json`. The log holds 12 new per-connection `sshd` processes, from 16:35:03Z to 16:41:19Z, and each one matched one of the spike's 12 `ssh` spawns in that span. No other connection arrived while the watcher ran. Every pid that `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/rec.cjs` recorded on Windows is in that set |
160	
161	On Windows every file sat in one directory, so the sources under
162	`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/win/` loaded a [name withheld]-identical copy of
163	`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/rec.cjs` beside them. `cmp` confirmed the two were
164	identical, and the copy is not [name withheld] twice.
165	
166	Round 2 differs from round 1 only in how it records:
167	
168	- The census and the macOS listing call `[name withheld] -O`, so [name withheld] does its kernel reads in its own
169	  process instead of forked helpers. The census reads the same fields as before.
170	- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/fixture.sh` ends with `exec sleep 300` instead of `sleep 300`, so its tail makes no new process.
171	- The listing runs in Node instead of `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/list.sh`.
172	- WSL was reached only through `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e`, as in round 1. The WSL machine booted again
173	  between sessions, because WSL stops when idle. The final check printed a boot time of 16:41:18Z,
174	  and its pids began again near 400.
175	
176	### What round 2 measured
177	
178	Every cell of the table below matches round 1. Times are in `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/transcript-r2.txt`.
179	
180	| Run | Ending | Reading at least 10 s after the ending |
181	|---|---|---|
182	| mr1, macOS | E1, process-group kill of 74511 at 16:33:16.190Z | count=3: the supervisor, M3 (74528) and M6 (74532) alive. M1, M2, M4 and M5 ended |
183	| mr2, macOS | [name withheld], group kill of 99236, then the census killed 99254 (M3) | count=2: the supervisor and M6 (99267) alive. M1 to M5 ended |

229	
230	## Recommendations
231	
232	### Linux under WSL2: a delegated cgroup per dispatch, ended by cgroup.kill
233	
234	The next card should do these things:
235	
236	- Obtain a delegated cgroup from the systemd user manager, with `systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes`
237	  or an equivalent D-Bus call.
238	- Give each dispatch a child cgroup in it, and place the dispatch's command there before the command
239	  starts anything.
240	- End the dispatch by writing cgroup.kill, and treat the cgroup as ended when cgroup.events
241	  reads `populated 0` or the cgroup is gone.
242	- Record the cgroup's path durably, so that the next start can write cgroup.kill to the orphan.
243	  That is the cgroup analogue of `R-STATE-10`, which w5's fresh writer showed works.
244	
245	This closed every escape the fixture tried, including M6, which today's route left alive (w2). It
246	ended M5 without a `SIGTERM` stage, and it took 0.88 to 1.66 ms. It needed no root.
247	
248	The next card must not assume the following:
249	
250	- That the systemd user manager exists. Here systemd was pid 1, and `[name withheld]` was running
251	  while a login session was open. Neither was tested without a session, nor with WSL's systemd
252	  turned off.
253	- That systemd's default `KillMode` is harmless. It sends `SIGTERM`, which M5 survived (w6).
254	- That a cgroup holds what an agent hands to another manager. A process the agent starts through
255	  `systemd-run --user`, or through D-Bus activation, lands in another unit. That was not measured.
256	  Moving a member out deliberately is outside this card.
257	- That WSL keeps a cgroup across WSL stopping. The brief says WSL stops when idle, and a cgroup does
258	  not survive that.
259	
260	**What would reverse it:**
261	
262	- A target host without the systemd user manager, or with it but without a delegated subtree.
263	- A kernel without cgroup.kill.
264	- An agent workload shown to escape the dispatch's cgroup through a route Rigger cannot close.
265	
266	In the first two cases, the fallback is today's route plus the gap at M6, or a route that needs
267	root.
268	
269	### Native Windows: a Job Object per dispatch, with kill-on-job-close
270	
271	The next card should do these things:
272	
273	- Create one job per dispatch from the supervisor's own process, through [name withheld] or an equivalent
274	  in-process [name withheld].
275	- Set `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, and set no breakaway limit.
276	- Assign the dispatch's root before it starts children. Node's `spawn` offers no suspended start,
277	  so this needs a gate like the fixture's, or `[name withheld]` with `CREATE_SUSPENDED` through the
278 [name withheld].
279	- End the dispatch with `[name withheld]`.
280	
281	Kill-on-job-close then ends the whole tree when the supervisor is killed outright (x3). That is
282	stronger than macOS and Linux, where E2 leaves the tree for the next start.
283	
284	The next card must not assume the following:
285	
286	- That a non-elevated supervisor can do the same. Only an elevated one was measured.
287	- That a job without kill-on-job-close can be reached after the supervisor dies. Its name goes with
288	  the last handle (x4), so a "next start ends the remains" design, as `R-STATE-10` has, does not
289	  carry over without a second handle holder. That was not measured.
290	- That the outer job a host puts Rigger in is benign. Under SSH, the session's job ended every member
291	  on disconnect, which is a containment Rigger did not choose.
292	
293	**What would reverse it:**
294	
295	- A non-elevated supervisor that cannot create or assign the job.
296	- An agent tool that starts processes with `CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB` under a job that allows it.
297	- Resume across a supervisor restart (the Temporal track) turning out to need dispatches that
298	  outlive the supervisor, because kill-on-job-close forbids exactly that.
299	
300	## What this would change, as a proposal
301	
302	This section is a proposal for the PM and the architect. The report changes none of these.
303	
304	| Document | What the recommendation would change |
305	|---|---|
306	| `D13` | Rule 1 makes macOS v0's only host and bars Windows-specific code before the WSL2 spike reports. A Linux or Windows host with its own containment backend is the deferred row "A native Windows host, with its own containment backend" |
307	| `R-STATE-17` | Containment is defined as a process group plus a census of the dispatch's directory. A cgroup or a job would be a containment unit that also holds M6, which the definition leaves outside |
308	| `R-STATE-9`, `R-STATE-10` and `R-STATE-11` | The killed-outright path: the next start ends each recorded process group, and must confirm that the group is still Rigger's. On Linux the recorded thing would be a cgroup path. On Windows, kill-on-job-close would end the tree at once, and nothing would be left for the next start |
309	| `R-STATE-19` and `R-STATE-26` | What Rigger records when it cannot read a group. A cgroup's cgroup.procs and a job's process list are different reads |
310	| `ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Failure model" | L0's containment paragraph: "A process that leaves its group is outside this containment" |
311	| `src/substrate/process.mjs` (`[name withheld]`) | The code the paragraph describes. This is a pointer for the architect, not a requirement |
312	
313	## `docs/v0-build-plan.md` §5
314	
315	This spike ran none of §5's steps. It ran no `wsl --status` or `wsl -l -v`, no [name withheld], `gh`, Claude
316	CLI or Tauri libraries, no Emend suite, no `cargo` build, no `claude -p` dispatch, and no Windows
317	native test comparison.
318	
319	It did unpack a pinned Node in WSL, which resembles part of step 2, but only as the fixture's
320	runtime, in the spike's own directory, and removed afterwards. It answers nothing of §5's verdict.
321	
322	## What failed
323	
324	- **The first WSL run lost its arguments.** `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e bash -lc "… e1 w1"` passed
325	  through cmd.exe, and the runner started with no arguments. It started a supervisor (pid 630)
326	  that crashed, and a unit `fx1430-.service` that failed. I then ended the runner (pid 618). From
327	  then on, every WSL command went in as a script on stdin.
328	- **A direct inspection command broke in cmd.exe.** cmd.exe split it at its pipes, and it
329	  returned `'fx-w1' is not recognized`. It was run outside the transcript wrapper, so the
330	  transcript's appendix records it.
331	- **w3's fresh-writer loop never ended.** It polled cgroup.events until `populated 0`, but systemd
332	  removed the cgroup once it emptied. The loop then spun on `grep` errors (20,209 lines) until I
333	  ended it (pid 2377). It forked many short processes, which is why WSL's pids jumped from about
334	  2,400 to 103,000. Its reading stood, because w3's listing at 00:51:55Z read `count=0`. w5 [name withheld]
335	  E2 with a loop that also stops when the cgroup is gone.
336	- **The first macOS run failed to start.** The worktree's `package.json` sets `"type": "module"`, so
337	  supervisor.js failed to load. It was renamed to `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/supervisor.cjs`.
338	- **`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/list.sh` printed a literal `3N` in its first timestamps**, because macOS `date` has no `%N`. It
339	  was fixed after m1. m1's listings carry .3NZ.
340	- **`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/runners/wsl-run.sh` relayed `$$` into a systemd `ExecStart` for w7.** systemd read it as a literal `$`,
341	  so the line `supervisor-process pid $` carries no pid. The `id` line above it is still the
342	  supervisor process's own, because `ExecStart` `exec`s into Node. The unit's main pid, 104328, is
343	  the supervisor's pid in the listing.
344	
345	- **The first commit was refused.** The transcript was first staged with a Markdown extension, and
346	  a test requires every tracked Markdown file under `docs/spikes/` to be a checked report. It is
347	  evidence, not a report, so it was renamed to
348	  `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/transcript.txt`.
349	- **Three more pre-commit refusals and one CI failure followed.**
350	  - Two [name withheld] bare file names in the report failed the path check.
351	  - A timing test in `test/loop.test.mjs` failed while the laptop's load average was about 100, and
352	    passed 3 of 3 times on its own.
353	  - CI's `fast` job then failed on the [name withheld] gitignored spike directory, which a clean checkout
354	    lacks. `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/ci-fast-136023691075-excerpt.txt` holds that output.
355	- **Round 2's first `npm install` took a marker argument as a package name** and failed with E404.
356	  It was rerun without the marker.
357	- **Round 2's first two WSL file copies wrote wrong bytes.** In the first, `rec` ran `base64` in the
358	  background without its here-document as stdin. In the second, the redirect of `rec`'s call also
359	  caught `rec`'s own records. The third copy's sha256 matched the laptop's for all five files, and
360	  only those copies ran.
361	- **Round 2's check A stopped the Windows watcher before the watcher had delivered check A's own
362	  start events.** That left four of check A's processes unrecorded ("Every process the spike
363	  started").
364	- **Round 2's first attempt at check B failed to start.** [name withheld]'s `echo` read `\c` in a comment as
365	  "stop output" and merged two lines, so Node raised `[name withheld]: list is not defined`. The
366	  retry wrote the script with Python.
367	
368	## Custody
369	
370	### The transcript
371	
372	No single file is a complete transcript. Four files hold the record, and each one's scope is
373	stated here:
374	
375	- **`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/session-log.txt`** holds the tool calls in the spike
376	  engineer's retained Claude Code session record, as of its generation at 2026-11-20T21:41:59Z: 216 calls with
377	  results, and a 217th (the call that generated it) with none. For each call it gives the input as
378	  the record holds it, the UTC times it was issued and returned, the host read from the command
379	  text, and the result text the record holds. It was generated by
380	  `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/gen-session-log.py`, and it replaces round 1's
381	  approximate appendix. It is limited in three ways:
382	  - Calls 25 and 207: the harness saved the result to a file, and the record holds only a preview
383	    and the file's path. Call 25's output is the 20,209 repeated `grep` lines, which
384	    `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/transcript.txt` collapses to a note. Call 207's output is
385	    the commit hook's suite output, which ended `pass 5965`, `fail 0`.
386	  - Calls 19, 46, 83, 95, 99, 103, 111, 113, 140, 206 and 208 ran in the background, and the
387	    record holds only their launch notice. What each one printed went to a file, and later calls
388	    read some of those files. Call 19's output is in `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/transcript.txt` at 00:45:14Z, and call 140's
389	    is in `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/transcript-r2.txt`. For the rest, the session
390	    log holds only the parts later calls read.
391	  - Anything that ran after 2026-11-20T21:41:59Z is not in it.
392	- **`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/transcript.txt`** holds only round 1's commands run through
393	  `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/runners/t.sh`, with their output. It carries two marked
394	  edits: a redaction, and the collapsed loop output.
395	- **`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/transcript-r2.txt`** holds only round 2's steps run
396	  through `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/orch.cjs`. For each it gives the exact argv,
397	  the stdin script with its sha256, the pids, and the step's stdout and stderr as captured. My
398	  other commands during round 2 are only in the session log.
399	- **`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/ci-fast-136023691075-excerpt.txt`** is an excerpt of the
400	  8 MB CI log of the failed `fast` job, with the log's sha256 and the command that fetches it.
401	
402	This evidence cannot be recovered. The report rests on none of it:
403	
404	- **The first pre-commit refusal (call 83).** The command kept only its last 15 lines
405	  (`| tail -15`). Those lines, and the reruns of the failing test that identified it, are in the
406	  session log.
407	- **The second and third refusals (calls 95 and 99).** Each went to a scratch file that the next

590	## Questions this leaves open, and what would settle them
591	
592	| Open question | What would settle it |
593	|---|---|
594	| Can a non-elevated Windows supervisor create and assign a job? | Run `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/` from a standard user's SSH session. That needs a non-admin account on the Windows machine, which is a host-configuration change for the owner to approve |
595	| Does the systemd user manager, and so the delegated cgroup, exist without an open login session? | Rerun w1 with every session closed and `loginctl enable-linger` unset. Enabling linger would be a configuration change |
596	| Does a second handle holder keep an orphaned Windows job reachable without kill-on-job-close? | One run with a holder process that opens the named job and outlives the supervisor |
597	| Does a real agent session stay in its cgroup or job? | That is outside this card, by its scope edges |

- **The Mac mini** is under the M8 coordinator's custody and is [name withheld]. Before any use, the spike tells rigger-ae on this card and waits for rigger-ae to relay the M8 coordinator's OK. Every command there runs through `~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/coordinator-tools/suite-slot.sh <tag> -- …`. The spike never touches the `judge-*` or `[name withheld]-*` clones there.

**Rules on how it runs.**

- **Where:** throwaway work lives in a directory named `<card>-spike-engineer`, where `<card>` is this issue's number: under the gitignored `spikes/` directory on a host with a Rigger checkout, and under a scratch directory of the spike's own making on any other host. It never merges (`AGENTS.md`, "When you write code"). Nothing runs Rigger from a checkout (`R-SAFE-5`).
- **Transcript:** the spike keeps a full transcript of every command it runs, each with its UTC time and host, and discloses every write it did not plan, with its reason (owner ruling [P32](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/642#issuecomment-6089559859)).
- **Stated intent:** before its first install or host-configuration change on any host, the spike posts a comment on this card listing each one it intends, with the host. A configuration change is anything outside its own directories that persists: a package install, a firewall rule, a WSL or systemd setting, a [name withheld] key.
- **Evidence:** every file the acceptance cites by path, except where an item says otherwise, is [name withheld] in the report's pull request under `docs/spikes/<report>/`, beside the report, so a judge opens it at the judged head. The transcript is among them.
- **Sibling spikes share the WSL2 machine.** The three Temporal-track spikes may run there at once. Before a WSL restart, a `.wslconfig` or `/etc/wsl.conf` change, or any other step that ends processes it did not start, a spike posts the step on all three cards and waits for each sibling spike, or rigger-ae, to answer that none of its recorded processes would be ended. #1429's Temporal server uses the default ports, 7233 and 8233. #1431's uses 7243 for [name withheld] and 8243 for the UI.
- **Processes:** the spike records the pid of every process it starts, and ends only those, or processes descended from them (`AGENTS.md`, "End only recorded processes").
- **Claude Code:** any live `claude` run uses a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` the spike made, never the owner's `~/.claude`.
- **M8 and the live board:** the spike writes nothing to the live Rigger board (project 6), to any card in the M8 milestone, or to any branch but this card's.
- **Temporal:** the local development server (`temporal server start-dev`) is the server for this track. Its docs say it is not intended for production use; this spike measures behaviour, not production fitness.

// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun: records every process this Node process starts (pid, command, times, exit) as JSON lines in $FX_REC.
'use strict';
const cp = require('node:child_process');
const fs = require('node:fs');
const os = require('node:os');
const FILE = process.env.FX_REC;
const HOST = process.env.FX_HOST || os.[name withheld]();
function note(o) {
  const line = JSON.stringify({ t: new Date().toISOString(), host: HOST, by: process.pid, ...o });
  if (FILE) fs.appendFileSync(FILE, line + '\n');
  return line;
}
note({ event: 'self', pid: process.pid, ppid: process.ppid, argv: process.argv });
function spawnSync(cmd, args, opts) {
  const started = new Date().toISOString();
  const r = cp.spawnSync(cmd, args, opts);
  note({ event: 'spawnSync', started, pid: r.pid, cmd, args, status: r.status, signal: r.signal });
  return r;
}
function spawn(cmd, args, opts) {
  const c = cp.spawn(cmd, args, opts);
  note({ event: 'spawn', pid: c.pid, cmd, args });
  c.on('exit', (code, sig) => note({ event: 'exit', pid: c.pid, code, sig }));
  return c;
}
module.exports = { spawnSync, spawn, note };
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun step runner on the laptop: runs one command (optionally fed a script on stdin), records its pid through
// rec.cjs, and appends the exact argv, stdin script, UTC start and end, host, exit and full output to transcript-r2.txt.
// Usage: FX_REC=... node orch.cjs <host-label> <stdin-file|-> -- <cmd> [args...]
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const crypto = require('node:crypto');
const rec = require('./rec.cjs');
const [host, input, sep, cmd, ...args] = process.argv.slice(2);
if (sep !== '--') throw new Error('usage');
const T = path.join(__dirname, 'transcript-r2.txt');
const start = new Date().toISOString();
const stdin = input === '-' ? null : fs.readFileSync(input);
const c = rec.spawn(cmd, args, { stdio: [stdin ? 'pipe' : 'ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'], env: process.env });
if (stdin) { c.stdin.end(stdin); }
let out = '';
c.stdout.on('data', (d) => { out += d; process.stdout.write(d); });
c.stderr.on('data', (d) => { out += d; process.stderr.write(d); });
c.on('close', (code, sig) => {
  const end = new Date().toISOString();
  const head = [`### ${start} -> ${end}  host=${host}  orch pid ${process.pid} (ppid ${process.ppid}), child pid ${c.pid}, exit ${code}${sig ? ' signal ' + sig : ''}`,
    '$ ' + JSON.stringify([cmd, ...args])];
  if (stdin) head.push(`stdin: ${input} (sha256 ${crypto.createHash('sha256').update(stdin).digest('hex')}):`, '<<<', stdin.toString().replace(/\n$/, ''), '>>>');
  fs.appendFileSync(T, '\n' + head.join('\n') + '\n--- output ---\n' + out.replace(/\n$/, '') + '\n');
  process.exit(code === null ? 1 : code);
});

#!/bin/bash
# ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun fixture (macOS and Linux): starts M1-M6 sleeping 300 s; every process it starts writes its pid to $FX_SHLOG.
# Usage: bash fixture.sh <run>, cwd = the dispatch directory. Only bash builtins run here between starts, so no unrecorded process is made.
R="$1"; L="$FX_SHLOG"
echo "root $$" >> "$L"
bash -c "echo M1 \$\$ >> '$L'; (exec -a fx-$R-M2 sleep 300) & echo M2 \$! >> '$L'; exec -a fx-$R-M1 sleep 300" &
echo "M1-from-root $!" >> "$L"
perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid() or die "setsid: $!"; exec {"sleep"} "fx-'"$R"'-M3", "300"' &
echo "M3 $!" >> "$L"
bash -c "echo M4-parent \$\$ >> '$L'; (exec -a fx-$R-M4 sleep 300) & echo M4 \$! >> '$L'; exit 0" &
echo "M4-parent-from-root $!" >> "$L"
bash -c "trap '' TERM; echo M5 \$\$ >> '$L'; exec -a fx-$R-M5 sleep 300" &
echo "M5-from-root $!" >> "$L"
perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid() or die "setsid: $!"; chdir "/" or die; exec {"sleep"} "fx-'"$R"'-M6", "300"' &
echo "M6 $!" >> "$L"
wait
exec sleep 300

// read with one ps, plus one python3 (macOS session ids) and one [name withheld] -O (macOS working directories), each recorded by rec.cjs.
'use strict';

const PY = '[name withheld]';
function list(run) {
  const [name withheld] = linux ? 'pid=,ppid=,pgid=,sid=,stat=,args=' : 'pid=,ppid=,pgid=,stat=,args=';
  const out = rec.spawnSync('ps', linux ? ['-eo', [name withheld]] : ['-axo', [name withheld]], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout;
  const re = new [name withheld](`fx-${run}-M|fixture\\.sh ${run}$|supervisor-posix\\.cjs \\w+ ${run} `);
  const rows = out.split('\n').filter((l) => re.test(l)).map((l) => {
    const f = l.trim().split(/\s+/);
    return linux ? { pid: f[0], ppid: f[1], pgid: f[2], sid: f[3], stat: f[4], args: f.slice(5).join(' ') }
      : { pid: f[0], ppid: f[1], pgid: f[2], stat: f[3], args: f.slice(4).join(' ') };
  });
  if (!linux && rows.length) {
    const s = rec.spawnSync(PY, ['-c', 'import os,sys\nfor p in sys.argv[1:]:\n  try: print(p, os.[name withheld](int(p)))\n  except Exception as e: print(p, "?")', ...rows.map((r) => r.pid)], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout;
    const l = rec.spawnSync('[name withheld]', ['-O', '-w', '-n', '-P', '-a', '-d', 'cwd', '-p', rows.map((r) => r.pid).join(','), '-[name withheld]'], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout;
    const sid = Object.[name withheld](s.trim().split('\n').map((x) => x.split(' ')));
    const cwd = {}; let p = null;
    for (const x of l.split('\n')) { if (x[0] === 'p') p = x.slice(1); if (x[0] === 'n') cwd[p] = x.slice(1); }
    for (const r of rows) { r.sid = sid[r.pid]; r.cwd = cwd[r.pid]; r.cgroup = '-'; }
  }
  if (linux) for (const r of rows) {
    try { r.cwd = fs.readlinkSync(`/proc/${r.pid}/cwd`); } catch { r.cwd = '?'; }
    try { r.cgroup = fs.readFileSync(`/proc/${r.pid}/cgroup`, 'utf8').trim().split(':')[2]; } catch { r.cgroup = '?'; }
  }
  const lines = [`listing run=${run} at ${new Date().toISOString()} on ${linux ? 'Linux' : '[name withheld]'}`,
    'PID     PPID    PGID    SID     STAT  CWD | CGROUP | ARGS'];
  for (const r of rows) lines.push(`${r.pid.padEnd(7)} ${r.ppid.padEnd(7)} ${r.pgid.padEnd(7)} ${String(r.sid).padEnd(7)} ${r.stat.padEnd(5)} ${r.cwd} | ${r.cgroup} | ${r.args}`);
  lines.push(`count=${rows.length}`);
  return { text: lines.join('\n'), rows };
}
module.exports = { list };

# fx1430r2 WSL setup: every external command runs through rec, which prints its pid; only bash builtins run otherwise.

# ABOUTME: Card #1430: turns the spike engineer's retained Claude Code session record (JSON lines) into a plain-text log of every tool
# call, with its UTC issue and completion times, the host it acted on, its exact input and its full result, redacting one account name.
import json, re, sys
src, dst = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
uses, results, order = {}, {}, []
for line in open(src):
    d = json.loads(line)
    if d.get('type') not in ('assistant', 'user') or not d.get('timestamp'):
        continue
    c = d.get('message', {}).get('content')
    if not isinstance(c, list):
        continue
    for x in c:
        if x.get('type') == 'tool_use' and x['id'] not in uses:
            uses[x['id']] = (d['timestamp'], x['name'], x['input']); order.append(x['id'])
        if x.get('type') == 'tool_result':
            body = x.get('content')
            if isinstance(body, list):
                body = '\n'.join(b.get('text', '[non-text block]') if isinstance(b, dict) else str(b) for b in body)
            results[x['tool_use_id']] = (d['timestamp'], bool(x.get('is_error')), body if body is not None else '')
def host(name, inp):
    if name != 'Bash':
        return 'laptop'
    cmd = inp.get('command', '')
    if 'wsl.exe' in cmd or 'wsl-hostpc' in cmd: return 'laptop -> hostpc -> WSL'
    if 'hostpc' in cmd: return 'laptop -> hostpc'
    return 'laptop'
def redact(s):
    s = re.sub(r'MicrosoftAccount\\\S+(\s+)User(\s+)S-1-11-96-[0-9-]+', lambda m: 'MicrosoftAccount\\[redacted: account name]' + m.group(1) + 'User' + m.group(2) + 'S-1-11-96-[redacted]', s)
    s = re.sub(r'[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[name withheld]', '[redacted: account name]', s)
    return s
out = ['ABOUTME: The tool calls in the #1430 spike engineer\'s retained Claude Code session record, with UTC times, host, input and the result text the record holds.', '',
       'Generated by gen-session-log.py from the session record of subagent a9a3ffa429348b341. Times are UTC: the first is when the call was issued, the second when its result returned.',
       '"host" is read from the command text: laptop unless the command reaches hostpc over ssh, and WSL when it runs wsl.exe. A command that ran in the background shows its launch result here; its output file is named in the result.',
       'Redactions: one Microsoft account name and its SID, wherever whoami /groups output appears. Nothing else is removed. A call that returned an error is marked ERROR.',
       'Scope: only calls the record holds up to the time it was generated. A result the harness saved to a file holds only a preview here, and a background command holds only its launch notice. The report, under "The transcript", lists those calls and what the log lacks.', '']
for n, i in enumerate(order, 1):
    t0, name, inp = uses[i]
    t1, err, body = results.get(i, (None, False, '[no result recorded: the session record ends before it]'))
    shown = inp.get('command') if name == 'Bash' and set(inp) <= {'command', 'description', 'timeout', 'run_in_background', 'dangerouslyDisableSandbox'} else json.dumps(inp, ensure_ascii=False, indent=1)
    extra = '' if name != 'Bash' else '  ' + json.dumps({k: v for k, v in inp.items() if k != 'command'}, ensure_ascii=False)
    out.append(f'### {n}. {t0} -> {t1}  host={host(name, inp)}  tool={name}{"  ERROR" if err else ""}{extra}')
    out.append(redact(shown if isinstance(shown, str) else str(shown)))
    out.append('--- result ---')
    out.append(redact(str(body)))
    out.append('')
open(dst, 'w').write('\n'.join(out))
print(len(order), 'calls')

**Stated intent (spike engineer, #1459)**, posted before the spike's first install or host-configuration change. **What the host already has (read-only probes p0b and p1, 2026-11-22T02:57Z and 02:59Z):** `loginctl show-user [name withheld]` printed `Linger=yes` and `State=lingering`; `[name withheld]` has mtime 2026-11-05 01:11:20Z, so linger was set before this spike. RL is therefore the host as found.

| # | Host | Change | Configuration change? | Revert and proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | WSL2 | Download the official Node v24.19.0 linux-x64 tarball and unpack it into `/home/[name withheld]/1459-spike-engineer/`, the spike's own directory, with the spike's scripts | No: it stays in the spike's own directory | Directory removed at the end; `ls -d` shows it gone |

| 6 | WSL2 | N2: open a `[name withheld]` login session through WSL interop (`wsl.exe -u [name withheld] -e sleep <n>` from inside the root script), then close it while the tree runs | No | Session listing shows it closed |

### 20. 2026-11-22T03:03:15.714Z -> 2026-11-22T03:03:15.853Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write WSL bash helper library"}
mkdir -p /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer/wsl && cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer/wsl && cat > lib.sh <<'[name withheld]'
# ABOUTME: Card #1459 helpers sourced by every root script in WSL: UTC timestamps, the process record (pid, starttime, boot id),
# login-state reads, and a scan that records every new process in user.slice, /non-systemd or an fx1459 unit.
export TZ=UTC LC_ALL=C
D=/home/[name withheld]/1459-spike-engineer
RECF=$D/record.txt
BOOT=$(</proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id)
while read -r k v; do [[ $k == btime ]] && BT=$v; done < /proc/stat
now(){ local t=$EPOCHREALTIME; printf '%(%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S)T.%sZ' "${t%.*}" "${t#*.}"; }
say(){ echo "$(now) $*"; }
declare -A SEEN
# rp <label> <pid>: record one process by pid and its starttime (field 22 of /proc/<pid>/stat, clock ticks since boot).
rp(){
  local raw; raw=$(</proc/$2/stat) 2>/dev/null || { say "REC-MISS label=$1 pid=$2 (gone before it could be read)"; return 0; }
  local rest=${raw##*) }; local -a f=($rest); local st=${f[19]}
  local key="$2:$st"; [[ -n ${SEEN[$key]} ]] && return 0; SEEN[$key]=1
  local cs=$(( BT*100 + st )); local line
  printf -v line 'REC host=wsl boot=%s pid=%s starttime=%s created=%(%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S)T.%02dZ recorded=%s label=%s' "$BOOT" "$2" "$st" "$((cs/100))" "$((cs%100))" "$(now)" "$1"
  echo "$line"; [[ -d $D ]] && echo "$line" >> "$RECF"
}
# recnew <label>: record every live process whose cgroup is in user.slice, /non-systemd or an fx1459 unit, not yet recorded.
recnew(){
  local p cg
  for p in /proc/[0-9]*; do
    p=${p#/proc/}; [[ $p == "$BASHPID" ]] && continue
    cg=$(</proc/$p/cgroup) 2>/dev/null || continue
    case $cg in *user.slice*|*non-systemd*|*fx1459*) rp "$1:$(tr '\0' ' ' < /proc/$p/cmdline 2>/dev/null | cut -c1-80)" "$p";; esac
  done
}
# recall <label>: record every live process (used when this script's own connection booted the distro).
recall(){ local p; for p in /proc/[0-9]*; do p=${p#/proc/}; [[ $p == "$BASHPID" ]] && continue; rp "$1:$(tr '\0' ' ' < /proc/$p/cmdline 2>/dev/null | cut -c1-80)" "$p"; done; }
# start_record: record this script, its /init parents, and either the whole boot (if this connection booted WSL) or the new processes.
start_record(){
  local raw; raw=$(</proc/$$/stat); local rest=${raw##*) }; local -a f=($rest); MYST=${f[19]}
  say "script pid $$ ppid $PPID starttime $MYST boot_id $BOOT btime $BT uptime $(cut -d' ' -f1 /proc/uptime)"
  rp "script:$TAG" $$; rp "init-parent" $PPID; local pp; pp=$(awk '{print $4}' /proc/$PPID/stat); rp "init-grandparent" "$pp"
  if (( MYST < 1500 )); then say "this connection booted the distro (script started ${MYST} ticks after boot): recording every process"; recall "boot"; else recnew "new"; fi
}
# winrec <label>: record, through interop, this connection's Windows process chain and any WSL VM process started since it began.
winrec(){ say "winrec $1"; /mnt/c/Windows/System32/WindowsPowerShell/v1.0/powershell.exe -NoProfile -NonInteractive -EncodedCommand "$(sed "s/__TAG__/$TAG/;s/__LABEL__/$1/" $D/winrec.ps1 | iconv -t UTF-16LE | base64 -w0)" | tr -d '\r' | tee -a "$RECF"; }
login_state(){
  say "LOGIN STATE ($1)"; echo '$ loginctl list-sessions --no-pager'; loginctl list-sessions --no-pager
  echo '$ loginctl show-user [name withheld] --no-pager -p State -p Sessions -p Linger -p Service'; loginctl show-user [name withheld] --no-pager -p State -p Sessions -p Linger -p Service 2>&1
  echo '$ systemctl is-active [name withheld]'; systemctl is-active [name withheld]
}
wsl_alive(){ say "WSL instance: boot_id $(</proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id) uptime $(cut -d' ' -f1 /proc/uptime) s ($1)"; }
# user_sessions: ids of [name withheld]'s sessions other than the user manager's own (class manager).
user_sessions(){ loginctl list-sessions --no-legend --no-pager | awk '$3=="[name withheld]" && $6!="manager" {print $1}'; }
# foreign_[name withheld] processes this spike did not record. Prints them; returns 0 if any exist.
foreign_[name withheld](){
  local p u any=1 raw rest st; local -a f
  for p in /proc/[0-9]*; do p=${p#/proc/}; u=$(stat -c %u /proc/$p 2>/dev/null) || continue; [[ $u == 1000 ]] || continue
    raw=$(</proc/$p/stat) 2>/dev/null || continue; rest=${raw##*) }; f=($rest); st=${f[19]}
    [[ -n ${SEEN[$p:$st]} ]] && [[ ${[name withheld][$p:$st]} != boot* ]] && continue
    case $(</proc/$p/cgroup) in *[name withheld]/init.scope*) continue;; esac
    echo "[name withheld] process not started by this script: pid $p $(tr '\0' ' ' < /proc/$p/cmdline | cut -c1-100) | $(</proc/$p/cgroup)"; any=0
  done; return $any
}
[name withheld]
echo ok
--- result ---
ok

### 21. 2026-11-22T03:03:36.617Z -> 2026-11-22T03:03:36.796Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Fix helper library and write Windows record script"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer/wsl && python3 - <<'PY'
import re
s=open('lib.sh').read()
s=s.replace('''  local raw; raw=$(</proc/$2/stat) 2>/dev/null || { say "REC-MISS label=$1 pid=$2 (gone before it could be read)"; return 0; }''','''  local raw; { raw=$(</proc/$2/stat); } 2>/dev/null || { say "REC-MISS label=$1 pid=$2 (gone before it could be read)"; return 0; }''')
s=s.replace('''    cg=$(</proc/$p/cgroup) 2>/dev/null || continue''','''    { cg=$(</proc/$p/cgroup); } 2>/dev/null || continue''')
i=s.index('# foreign_[name withheld]')
s=s[:i]+'''# foreign_[name withheld] processes outside the user manager's own init.scope and outside this spike's fx1459 units. Prints them; returns 0 if any.
foreign_[name withheld](){
  local p u cg any=1
  for p in /proc/[0-9]*; do p=${p#/proc/}; u=$(stat -c %u /proc/$p 2>/dev/null) || continue; [[ $u == 1000 ]] || continue
    { cg=$(</proc/$p/cgroup); } 2>/dev/null || continue
    case $cg in *[name withheld]/init.scope*|*fx1459*) continue;; esac
    echo "[name withheld] process outside the manager and the spike's units: pid $p $(tr '\\0' ' ' < /proc/$p/cmdline 2>/dev/null | cut -c1-100) | $cg"; any=0
  done; return $any
}
'''
open('lib.sh','w').write(s)
PY
cat > winrec.ps1 <<'[name withheld]'
# ABOUTME: Card #1459 Windows process record, run through WSL interop: this connection's chain from its per-connection sshd down, and any WSL VM process started since that sshd.
$tag = '__TAG__'; $label = '__LABEL__'
$all = @(Get-CimInstance Win32_Process)
$now = (Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString('yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ss.fffZ')
$by = @{}; foreach ($p in $all) { $by[[int]$p.ProcessId] = $p }
$w = $all | Where-Object { $_.Name -eq 'wsl.exe' -and $_.CommandLine -like "*$tag*" } | Sort-Object CreationDate | Select-Object -First 1
if (-not $w) { "REC-WIN-NONE no wsl.exe carrying $tag"; exit }

$queue.Enqueue($root)
foreach ($p in $all) { if ($p.Name -match '^(vmwp\.exe|vmmemWSL|wslrelay\.exe|wslhost\.exe)$' -and $p.CreationDate -ge $root.CreationDate) { $queue.Enqueue($p) } }
while ($queue.Count) { $p = $queue.Dequeue(); if ($set.ContainsKey([int]$p.ProcessId)) { continue }; $set[[int]$p.ProcessId] = $p
  foreach ($c in $all) { if ([int]$c.ParentProcessId -eq [int]$p.ProcessId -and $c.CreationDate -ge $p.CreationDate) { $queue.Enqueue($c) } } }
foreach ($p in ($set.Values | Sort-Object CreationDate)) {
  $cl = ($p.CommandLine -replace '\s+', ' '); if ($cl.Length -gt 90) { $cl = $cl.Substring(0, 90) }
  "REC host=windows pid={0} ppid={1} created={2} recorded={3} label={4}:{5}:{6}" -f $p.ProcessId, $p.ParentProcessId, $p.CreationDate.ToUniversalTime().ToString('yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ss.fffZ'), $now, $label, $p.Name, $cl

### 22. 2026-11-22T03:03:47.301Z -> 2026-11-22T03:03:47.450Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write recording fixture script"}

#!/bin/bash
# ABOUTME: Card #1459 fixture (#1430's members M1-M6, sleeping 300 s): every process it starts is recorded, as it starts, with its
# pid and /proc starttime, by the process that starts it, using bash builtins only. Usage: bash fixture.sh <run>, cwd = dispatch dir.
R="$1"; L="$FX_SHLOG"; export TZ=UTC
r(){ local raw; { raw=$(</proc/$2/stat); } 2>/dev/null || { echo "MISS $1 $2" >> "$L"; return; }; local rest=${raw##*) }; local -a f=($rest); local t=$EPOCHREALTIME; printf 'MEMBER %s %s %s %(%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S)T.%sZ\n' "$1" "$2" "${f[19]}" "${t%.*}" "${t#*.}" >> "$L"; }
R_DEF="$(declare -f r)"
r root $$
bash -c "$R_DEF; L='$L'; r M1 \$\$; (exec -a fx-$R-M2 sleep 300) & r M2 \$!; exec -a fx-$R-M1 sleep 300" &
r M1-from-root $!
perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid() or die "setsid: $!"; exec {"sleep"} "fx-'"$R"'-M3", "300"' &
r M3 $!
bash -c "$R_DEF; L='$L'; r M4-parent \$\$; (exec -a fx-$R-M4 sleep 300) & r M4 \$!; exit 0" &
r M4-parent-from-root $!
bash -c "trap '' TERM; $R_DEF; L='$L'; r M5 \$\$; exec -a fx-$R-M5 sleep 300" &
r M5-from-root $!
perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid() or die "setsid: $!"; chdir "/" or die; exec {"sleep"} "fx-'"$R"'-M6", "300"' &
r M6 $!
wait
exec sleep 300

### 23. 2026-11-22T03:04:05.244Z -> 2026-11-22T03:04:05.564Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write member listing module"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer/wsl && cat > list.cjs <<'[name withheld]'
// ABOUTME: Card #1459 listing: for each fixture member a run recorded (pid and starttime), whether that exact process is alive, read
// from /proc with no child process; and any other live process whose argv names the run. Usable as a module or as `node list.cjs <run> [label]`.
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const D = '/home/[name withheld]/1459-spike-engineer';
function stat(pid) {
  try { const raw = fs.readFileSync(`/proc/${pid}/stat`, 'utf8'); const f = raw.slice(raw.lastIndexOf(') ') + 2).split(' '); return { state: f[0], ppid: f[1], pgid: f[2], sid: f[3], start: f[19] }; } catch { return null; }
}
function rd(p, fn) { try { return fn(p); } catch { return '?'; } }
function members(run) {
  const f = path.join(D, 'runs', run, 'members.txt');
  const out = [];
  for (const l of (fs.existsSync(f) ? fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8') : '').split('\n')) {
    const m = l.match(/^MEMBER (\S+) (\d+) (\d+) (\S+)$/); if (m && !/from-root/.test(m[1])) out.push({ who: m[1], pid: m[2], start: m[3] });
  }
  return out;
}
function list(run, label) {
  const lines = [`listing run=${run} (${label}) at ${new Date().toISOString()} by pid ${process.pid} uid ${process.getuid()}`,
    'WHO        PID     START     ALIVE STATE PPID    PGID    SID     CWD | CGROUP | ARGS'];
  const seen = new Set(); let alive = 0; const ms = members(run);
  for (const m of ms) {
    const s = stat(m.pid); const same = s && s.start === m.start && s.state !== 'Z';
    seen.add(m.pid + ':' + m.start); if (same && m.who !== 'M4-parent') alive++;
    const extra = same ? `${s.state.padEnd(5)} ${s.ppid.padEnd(7)} ${s.pgid.padEnd(7)} ${s.sid.padEnd(7)} ${rd(`/proc/${m.pid}/cwd`, fs.readlinkSync)} | ${rd(`/proc/${m.pid}/cgroup`, (p) => fs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8').trim().split(':')[2])} | ${rd(`/proc/${m.pid}/cmdline`, (p) => fs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8').split('\0').join(' ').trim())}`
      : (s ? `-     (pid ${m.pid} now has starttime ${s.start} state ${s.state}: not the recorded process)` : '-     (no such pid)');
    lines.push(`${m.who.padEnd(10)} ${m.pid.padEnd(7)} ${m.start.padEnd(9)} ${same ? 'yes' : 'NO '}   ${extra}`);
  }
  const others = [];
  for (const p of fs.readdirSync('/proc').filter((x) => /^\d+$/.test(x))) {
    const cmd = rd(`/proc/${p}/cmdline`, (q) => fs.readFileSync(q, 'utf8').split('\0').join(' ').trim());
    if (!cmd.includes(`fx-${run}-`) && !cmd.includes(`fixture.sh ${run}`)) continue;
    const s = stat(p); if (!s || seen.has(p + ':' + s.start)) continue;
    others.push(`unrecorded live process naming the run: pid ${p} start ${s.start} state ${s.state} | ${cmd}`);
  }
  lines.push(...others);
  lines.push(`members recorded=${ms.filter((m) => m.who !== 'M4-parent').length} alive=${alive} (root and M1-M6; M4-parent exits at once by design) unrecorded-naming-run=${others.length}`);
  return { text: lines.join('\n'), alive, total: ms.filter((m) => m.who !== 'M4-parent').length };
}
module.exports = { list, members };
if (require.main === module) console.log(list(process.argv[2], process.argv[3] || 'driver').text);

}

        'recnew "end"', 'winrec s1-end', 'say "s1 done"']
open('s1-setup.sh', 'w').write('\n'.join(out) + '\n')
print('ok')

cat > gen-s2.py <<'PY'
# ABOUTME: Card #1459: builds WSL step s2: the helper library inline, the s2 body, the updated spike files as base64, and the interop login probe.
import base64, hashlib, os
W = 'wsl'
files = ['lib.sh', 'fixture.sh', 'list.cjs', 'supervisor.cjs', 'writer.cjs', 'winrec.ps1']
out = [open(os.path.join(W, 'lib.sh')).read(), open('s2-body.sh').read()]
for f in files:
    data = open(os.path.join(W, f), 'rb').read(); b = base64.b64encode(data).decode()
    out.append(f"base64 -d > $D/{f} <<'B64'\n" + '\n'.join(b[i:i+76] for i in range(0, len(b), 76)) + '\nB64')
    out.append(f'echo "laptop sha256 {hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()}  {f}"')
out.append(open('s2-tail.sh').read())
open('s2.sh', 'w').write('\n'.join(out) + '\n'); print('ok')
PY

/mnt/c/Windows/System32/wsl.exe -u $TU -e sleep 6 &
H=$!; rp "interop wsl.exe shim for the $TU login" $H
for i in $(seq 1 30); do
  sleep 1; recnew "probe-$i"
  echo "$(now) t+${i}s holder $(kill -0 $H 2>/dev/null && echo alive || echo exited) sessions: $(loginctl list-sessions --no-legend --no-pager | awk -v u=$TU '$3==u {printf "%s(%s,leader %s) ", $1, $6, $5}') user@$TUID: $(systemctl is-active user@$TUID.service)"
done
wait $H; echo "holder exit $?"
login_state "30 s after the interop login began"
echo '$ journalctl --no-pager -o short-iso-precise _SYSTEMD_UNIT=systemd-logind.service --since "-60s"'; journalctl --no-pager -o short-iso-precise _SYSTEMD_UNIT=systemd-logind.service --since "-60s" | tail -20
echo '$ ps -o pid,ppid,user,lstart,cgroup:60,args -u rigger-spike'; ps -o pid,ppid,user,lstart,cgroup:60,args -u rigger-spike
recnew "end"; winrec s2-end; wsl_alive end; say "s2 done"
