### 46. 2026-11-22T03:47:15.452Z -> 2026-11-22T03:47:15.805Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write the case driver script"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer && cat > case-body.sh <<'[name withheld]'
# ---- case driver body. Arguments: <tag> <route r0|rl|rs> <cases...>. Runs each case for the user under test, as root only to
# set up or revert the route and to observe; every supervisor and fresh writer runs as the user under test.
TAG=$1; ROUTE=$2; shift 2; CASES="$*"
start_record; winrec start; wsl_alive start
UNITF=/etc/systemd/system/fx1459-rs@.service
asuser(){ setpriv --reuid=$TUID --regid=$TUID --init-groups env -i HOME=/home/$TU USER=$TU LOGNAME=$TU PATH=/usr/bin:/bin XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/$TUID "$@"; }
revert(){
  case $ROUTE in
    rl) say '$ loginctl disable-linger rigger-spike'; loginctl disable-linger $TU; echo "exit $?"; ls -la /var/lib/systemd/linger;;
    rs) say "revert RS"; systemctl stop 'fx1459-rs@*.service' 2>&1; systemctl reset-failed 'fx1459-rs@*.service' 2>&1; rm -v $UNITF; systemctl daemon-reload; echo '$ systemctl cat fx1459-rs@x.service'; systemctl cat fx1459-rs@x.service 2>&1; ls -la $UNITF 2>&1;;
  esac
}
say "=== ROUTE $ROUTE setup"
login_state "route $ROUTE, before setup"
case $ROUTE in
  r0) echo "R0: the user as created, linger unset"; ls -la /var/lib/systemd/linger;;
  rl) trap revert EXIT; say '$ loginctl enable-linger rigger-spike'; loginctl enable-linger $TU; echo "exit $?"; ls -la /var/lib/systemd/linger
      for i in $(seq 1 50); do [[ $(systemctl is-active user@$TUID.service) == active ]] && break; sleep 0.2; done; say "user@$TUID: $(systemctl is-active user@$TUID.service)"; recnew "rl-setup";;
  rs) trap revert EXIT; say "install $UNITF"
      cat > $UNITF <<UNIT
# ABOUTME: Card #1459 route RS: a system unit that runs the supervisor as the unprivileged user under test with a delegated cgroup.
[Unit]
Description=fx1459 RS supervisor %i
[Service]
Type=simple
User=$TU
Group=$TU
Delegate=yes
KillMode=process
WorkingDirectory=$D
ExecStart=$NODE $D/supervisor.cjs %i rs-%i
StandardOutput=append:$D/runs/rs-%i/sup.log
StandardError=append:$D/runs/rs-%i/sup.log
UNIT
      cat $UNITF; systemctl daemon-reload; echo "daemon-reload exit $?";;
esac
waitlog(){ local f=$1 pat=$2 n=$3; for ((i=0;i<n*10;i++)); do grep -qE "$pat" $f 2>/dev/null && return 0; sleep 0.1; done; return 1; }
alive_rec(){ st $1 && [[ ${STF[19]} == "$2" && ${STF[0]} != Z ]]; }
supinfo(){ local l; l=$(grep -m1 '^REC .*label=supervisor:' $R/sup.log); SUP=$(sed -E 's/.* pid=([0-9]+) .*/\1/' <<<"$l"); SUPST=$(sed -E 's/.* starttime=([0-9]+) .*/\1/' <<<"$l"); TREE=$(grep -m1 ' TREE ' $R/sup.log | awk '{print $3}'); }
listing(){ asuser $NODE $D/list.cjs $RUN "driver: $1" 2>&1; }
end_survivors(){
  local who pid start
  while read -r _ who pid start _; do [[ $who == *from-root* ]] && continue; if alive_rec $pid $start; then kill -9 $pid && say "cleanup: ended recorded $who pid $pid (starttime $start)"; fi; done < <(grep '^MEMBER' $R/members.txt 2>/dev/null)
  [[ -n $SUP ]] && alive_rec $SUP $SUPST && kill -9 $SUP && say "cleanup: ended recorded supervisor $SUP"
}
run_case(){
  local c=$1 mode=$1; RUN=$ROUTE-$c; R=$D/runs/$RUN; SUP=; SUPST=; TREE=; H=
  mkdir -p $R; chown $TU:$TU $R
  say "=== CASE $c under $ROUTE (run $RUN)"; wsl_alive "case $RUN start"
  if [[ $c == n2 ]]; then
    say "N2: open a $TU login session through WSL interop: wsl.exe -u $TU -e sleep 8"
    /mnt/c/Windows/System32/wsl.exe -u $TU -e sleep 8 & H=$!; rp "interop wsl.exe shim, $RUN login" $H
    for i in $(seq 1 50); do [[ -n $(tu_sessions) ]] && break; sleep 0.2; done; recnew "$RUN-login"
    login_state "$RUN: session open, before supervisor start"
  else
    login_state "$RUN: during the run, before supervisor start"
    if [[ -n $(tu_sessions) ]]; then say "ABORT $RUN: $TU has a login session open"; return; fi
  fi
  if [[ $ROUTE == r0 && $c != n2 ]]; then mode=obtain; fi
  say "start supervisor (mode $mode)"
  case $ROUTE in
    r0) if [[ $c == n2 ]]; then
          echo "\$ asuser systemd-run --user --unit=fx1459-$RUN -p Delegate=yes -p KillMode=process -p StandardOutput=append:$R/sup.log -p StandardError=append:$R/sup.log --working-directory=$D $NODE $D/supervisor.cjs $mode $RUN"
          asuser systemd-run --user --unit=fx1459-$RUN -p Delegate=yes -p KillMode=process -p StandardOutput=append:$R/sup.log -p StandardError=append:$R/sup.log --working-directory=$D $NODE $D/supervisor.cjs $mode $RUN 2>&1; echo "exit $?"
        else
          echo "\$ asuser $NODE $D/supervisor.cjs obtain $RUN > $R/sup.log 2>&1   (started directly as $TU with no session: R0 changes nothing)"
          asuser $NODE $D/supervisor.cjs obtain $RUN > $R/sup.log 2>&1; echo "exit $?"
          echo '$ asuser systemd-run --user --unit=fx1459-'$RUN'-unit -p Delegate=yes --working-directory='$D' '$NODE' '$D'/supervisor.cjs obtain '$RUN; asuser systemd-run --user --unit=fx1459-$RUN-unit -p Delegate=yes --working-directory=$D $NODE $D/supervisor.cjs obtain $RUN 2>&1; echo "exit $?"
        fi;;
    rl) echo "\$ asuser systemd-run --user --unit=fx1459-$RUN -p Delegate=yes -p KillMode=process -p StandardOutput=append:$R/sup.log -p StandardError=append:$R/sup.log --working-directory=$D $NODE $D/supervisor.cjs $mode $RUN"
        asuser systemd-run --user --unit=fx1459-$RUN -p Delegate=yes -p KillMode=process -p StandardOutput=append:$R/sup.log -p StandardError=append:$R/sup.log --working-directory=$D $NODE $D/supervisor.cjs $mode $RUN 2>&1; echo "exit $?";;
    rs) echo "\$ systemctl start fx1459-rs@$c.service"; systemctl start fx1459-rs@$c.service; echo "exit $?";;
  esac
  waitlog $R/sup.log 'READY|RESULT' 30; recnew "$RUN-supervisor"
  say "supervisor log so far:"; cat $R/sup.log; supinfo
  if grep -q 'RESULT' $R/sup.log; then say "RESULT for $RUN: $(grep RESULT $R/sup.log)"; recnew "$RUN-end"; return; fi
  [[ -z $SUP ]] && { say "no supervisor record; abandoning $RUN"; end_survivors; return; }
  say "supervisor pid $SUP starttime $SUPST tree $TREE"
  case $c in
    n1) login_state "$RUN: tree running, before the cgroup.kill write"
        touch $R/go; say "trigger written"; waitlog $R/sup.log 'DONE' 40; say "supervisor log:"; sed -n '/trigger seen/,$p' $R/sup.log
        listing "after DONE, at least 12 s after the write";;
    n3) login_state "$RUN: tree running, before the kill"
        listing "before kill -9 of the supervisor"
        if alive_rec $SUP $SUPST; then say "\$ kill -9 $SUP"; kill -9 $SUP; say "kill -9 sent to recorded supervisor $SUP"; fi
        sleep 12; listing "12 s after kill -9 of the supervisor, before the fresh write"
        login_state "$RUN: supervisor killed, before the fresh write"
        echo "\$ asuser $NODE $D/writer.cjs $RUN $TREE"; asuser $NODE $D/writer.cjs $RUN $TREE 2>&1
        sleep 12; listing "12 s after the fresh write";;
    n2) say "supervisor started under the open session; now close every session of $TU"
        wait $H; say "interop login command exited ($?); sessions of $TU: $(tu_sessions | tr '\n' ' ')"
        sleep 2; for s in $(tu_sessions); do say "\$ loginctl terminate-session $s   (its leader is the spike's own login, recorded above)"; loginctl terminate-session $s; echo "exit $?"; done
        for i in $(seq 1 50); do [[ -z $(tu_sessions) ]] && break; sleep 0.2; done
        CLOSED=$(now); say "every session of $TU closed at $CLOSED (tu_sessions empty: $([[ -z $(tu_sessions) ]] && echo yes || echo no))"
        for i in $(seq 1 30); do sleep 1; a=$(asuser $NODE $D/list.cjs $RUN watch | sed -n 's/.*alive=\([0-9]*\).*/\1/p'); echo "$(now) t+${i}s after close: members alive=$a supervisor $(alive_rec $SUP $SUPST && echo alive || echo gone) user@$TUID $(systemctl is-active user@$TUID.service)"; done
        login_state "$RUN: 30 s after every session closed, before the cgroup.kill write"
        listing "30 s after every session closed, before the cgroup.kill write"
        if alive_rec $SUP $SUPST; then touch $R/go; say "trigger written"; waitlog $R/sup.log 'DONE' 40; say "supervisor log:"; sed -n '/trigger seen/,$p' $R/sup.log
        else say "supervisor $SUP is gone; supervisor log:"; cat $R/sup.log
          if [[ -d $TREE ]]; then echo "\$ asuser $NODE $D/writer.cjs $RUN $TREE"; asuser $NODE $D/writer.cjs $RUN $TREE 2>&1; else say "tree cgroup $TREE does not exist: \$ ls -ld $TREE"; ls -ld $TREE 2>&1; fi
          sleep 12; fi
        listing "at least 12 s after the ending"
        echo "\$ journalctl --no-pager -o short-iso-precise --since @${CASEST} _SYSTEMD_UNIT=systemd-logind.service + UNIT=user@$TUID.service + _SYSTEMD_UNIT=user@$TUID.service"
        journalctl --no-pager -o short-iso-precise --since @${CASEST} _SYSTEMD_UNIT=systemd-logind.service + UNIT=user@$TUID.service + _SYSTEMD_UNIT=user@$TUID.service 2>&1 | tail -60;;
  esac
  recnew "$RUN-end"
  end_survivors
  [[ $ROUTE == rs ]] && { systemctl stop fx1459-rs@$c.service 2>/dev/null; systemctl reset-failed fx1459-rs@$c.service 2>/dev/null; }
  [[ $ROUTE != rs ]] && systemctl is-active user@$TUID.service >/dev/null && asuser systemctl --user reset-failed fx1459-$RUN.service 2>/dev/null
  sleep 1; listing "after cleanup"; wsl_alive "case $RUN end"
}
for c in $CASES; do CASEST=${EPOCHREALTIME%.*}; run_case $c; done
recnew "end"; login_state "route $ROUTE, end"; winrec end; wsl_alive end; say "driver done"
[name withheld]
cat lib-check 2>/dev/null; cat wsl/lib.sh case-body.sh > case.sh && bash -n case.sh && echo syntax ok

syntax ok

head, rest = s.split('TAG=$1; ROUTE=$2; shift 2; CASES="$*"\n',1)

ABOUTME: Journal for #1430: whether cgroup v2 under WSL2 and Job Objects on Windows end an agent's whole
process tree, against the macOS [name withheld] and today's route.

# #1430 — Whole-tree containment on Linux under WSL2 and on Windows

## What ran

On 2026-11-20 a throwaway fixture ran on three hosts: macOS on the laptop, Ubuntu 26.04 under WSL2,
and native Windows. The fixture was six sleeping members, two of which left the session and one of
which moved to `/`. Each host ran it under each ending the card names. The code stayed in the
gitignored `spikes/`. Its source, every listing and the transcript are in
[the report](../spikes/whole-tree-containment.md).

## What we learned

- **A cgroup cost nothing on this WSL2 host.** systemd was pid 1, and the user manager handed an
  unprivileged user a writable cgroup through `systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes`. `cgroup.kill`
  ended every member in 1.66 ms. An orphaned cgroup stayed reachable by its path after the
  supervisor died.
- **The shell `wsl.exe -e` starts is outside systemd's tree.** It sits in `/non-systemd`, owned by
  root. A delegated cgroup needs the user manager to start the process, or a scope to move it.
- **A Windows job's name dies with its last handle, not with the job.** Without kill-on-job-close,
  the members outlived the supervisor in a job that no fresh process could open.
  `[name withheld]` failed with error 2.
- **Today's route misses a process that leaves the session and the dispatch directory together.**
  M6 survived the group kill and the census on macOS and on Linux alike.
- **The SSH session is itself a job on Windows,** and it ended every survivor on disconnect.
  Windows readings taken over SSH must finish inside one session.
- **systemd's default `KillMode` sends `SIGTERM`,** which the member that ignores it survived.

## What failed

- **`cmd.exe` ate the arguments of the first WSL run,** and broke one inspection command at its
  pipes. After that, every WSL command went in as a script on stdin.
- **A polling loop waited for `populated 0` from a cgroup that systemd had removed,** and spun until
  I ended it. WSL's pid counter advanced by about 101,000 while it ran (2377 to 103714). The rerun's loop also stops when the
  cgroup is gone.
- **`supervisor.js` would not load,** because the repository's `package.json` sets
  `"type": "module"`. Throwaway [name withheld] in `spikes/` needs the `.cjs` extension.
- **A non-elevated Windows run could not be made.** `[name withheld]` failed over SSH, so every
  Windows reading is from an elevated supervisor.

## Round 2

- **"Every process" means every pid, and that has to be designed in before the run.**
  - Round 1 recorded only the long-lived processes, and the judge was right that synchronous
    helpers and M4's parent are processes too.
  - Round 2 recorded each spawn in Node, made the fixture write its own pids, and ran a [name withheld]
    process-start watcher on Windows.
  - Even so, the measuring sessions themselves cannot be fully recorded, and the report names what
    is left.
- **A watcher must outlive the events it is meant to catch.** Check A stopped the Windows watcher
  before [name withheld] had delivered check A's own start events.
- **A command log is [name withheld] to produce from the session record.** Round 1's hand-kept
  appendix could only give time windows. The retained tool-call record gives exact inputs, times
  and outputs.
ea5aef2c Round 3 of #1430: commit the fixture pid logs and state each record's scope
7358dad3 Round 2 of #1430: full command log and every process recorded
3791412d Write the gitignored spike directory in prose in the #1430 report

### 78. 2026-11-22T04:23:56.395Z -> 2026-11-22T04:23:56.846Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  ERROR  {"description": "Build process record from transcript"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer && /usr/bin/grep -a -c '^=== FILE' out-s3.txt; /usr/bin/grep -a '^=== FILE' out-s3.txt | cut -c1-150; cat > gen-record.py <<'PY'
# ABOUTME: Card #1459: builds the process record from the step transcript: every REC line (WSL and Windows) and every fixture MEMBER
# line, with the step it came from; MEMBER lines take their boot id from the run's supervisor record.
import re, sys, collections
t = open('transcript.txt', encoding='utf-8', errors='replace').read().split('\n')
step = None; out = []; seen = set(); runboot = {}
for l in t:
    m = re.match(r'### step (\S+)', l)
    if m: step = m.group(1); continue
    m = re.match(r'REC host=wsl boot=(\S+) pid=(\d+) starttime=(\d+).*label=supervisor:(\S+)', l)
    if m: runboot[m.group(4)] = m.group(1)
cur_run = None
for l in t:
    m = re.match(r'### step (\S+)', l)
    if m: step = m.group(1); continue
    m = re.match(r'=== FILE \S+/runs/([^/]+)/members.txt', l)
    if m: cur_run = m.group(1); continue
    if l.startswith('=== END'): cur_run = None; continue
    if l.startswith('REC host='):
        k = re.sub(r' recorded=\S+', '', l)
        if k in seen: continue
        seen.add(k); out.append(f'step={step} {l}')
    m = re.match(r'MEMBER (\S+) (\d+) (\d+) (\S+)$', l)
    if m and cur_run:
        k = ('M', cur_run, m.group(2), m.group(3))
        if k in seen: continue
        seen.add(k)
        out.append(f'step={step} REC host=wsl boot={runboot.get(cur_run, "?")} pid={m.group(2)} starttime={m.group(3)} recorded={m.group(4)} label=member:{cur_run}:{m.group(1)} (written by the process that started it, into the run\'s members.txt)')
open(sys.argv[1], 'w').write('\n'.join(out) + '\n')
c = collections.Counter(re.search(r'host=(\w+)', x).group(1) for x in out)
print(len(out), dict(c))
PY
python3 gen-record.py record-draft.txt; /usr/bin/grep -c 'boot=?' record-draft.txt

### 79. 2026-11-22T04:24:09.325Z -> 2026-11-22T04:24:12.475Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Final WSL listing checked against the record"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer && python3 - <<'PY'
import re
trip=sorted({(m.group(1),m.group(2),m.group(3)) for l in open('record-draft.txt') for m in [re.search(r'host=wsl boot=(\S+) pid=(\d+) starttime=(\d+)',l)] if m})
body='''# fx1459 f2: final WSL listing after the last run. Lists every process with pid and creation time (boot id + /proc starttime, and
# ps lstart), then checks each of the spike's recorded WSL processes (boot id, pid, starttime) against it. Starts nothing that stays.
main(){
export TZ=UTC LC_ALL=C
B=$(</proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id); echo "date $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S.%NZ) boot_id $B uptime $(cut -d' ' -f1 /proc/uptime)"
echo "--- every process: PID STARTTIME(ticks since boot) LSTART ARGS"
for p in /proc/[0-9]*; do p=${p#/proc/}; { raw=$(</proc/$p/stat); } 2>/dev/null || continue; f=(${raw##*) }); echo "$p ${f[19]} $(ps -o lstart= -p $p 2>/dev/null) $(tr '\\0' ' ' < /proc/$p/cmdline 2>/dev/null | cut -c1-100)"; done
echo "--- recorded entries checked: boot pid starttime"
n=0; hit=0
while read -r b p s; do n=$((n+1)); if [[ $b == "$B" ]] && { raw=$(</proc/$p/stat); } 2>/dev/null; then f=(${raw##*) }); [[ ${f[19]} == "$s" ]] && { echo "MATCH $b $p $s"; hit=$((hit+1)); }; fi; done <<'LIST'
''' + '\n'.join(' '.join(t) for t in trip) + '''
LIST
echo "checked $n recorded WSL entries against boot $B: $hit match on boot, pid and starttime"
echo "date end $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S.%NZ)"
}
main "$@" < /dev/null
'''
open('f2.sh','w').write(body); print(len(trip))
PY

### 80. 2026-11-22T04:24:25.096Z -> 2026-11-22T04:24:26.079Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Build final Windows listing script; check WSL state"}

import re
rows=sorted({(m.group(1),m.group(2)) for l in open('record-draft.txt') for m in [re.search(r'host=windows pid=(\d+) ppid=\d+ created=(\S+)',l)] if m})
ps='''# fx1459 f1: final Windows listing after the last run: every process with pid and creation time, then each recorded Windows entry checked on pid and creation time.

"date " + (Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString('o') + "  processes: " + $all.Count
"--- every process: PID CREATED(UTC) NAME"
foreach ($p in ($all | Sort-Object ProcessId)) { "{0} {1} {2}" -f $p.ProcessId, $(if ($p.CreationDate) { $p.CreationDate.ToUniversalTime().ToString('yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ss.fffZ') } else { '-' }), $p.Name }
$rec = @(
''' + ',\n'.join(f"'{p} {c}'" for p,c in rows) + '''
)

"checked " + $rec.Count + " recorded Windows entries: " + $hit + " match on pid and creation time"
'''
open('f1.ps1','w').write(ps); print(len(rows))

### 98. 2026-11-22T04:35:31.518Z -> 2026-11-22T04:35:32.803Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Make N2 abort without a session; post recreate notice"}

s=open('case-body.sh').read()
old='''    for i in $(seq 1 50); do [[ -n $(tu_sessions) ]] && break; sleep 0.2; done; recnew "$RUN-login"
    login_state "$RUN: session open, before supervisor start"'''
new='''    for i in $(seq 1 100); do [[ -n $(tu_sessions) ]] && break; sleep 0.2; done; recnew "$RUN-login"
    login_state "$RUN: session open, before supervisor start"
    if [[ -z $(tu_sessions) ]]; then say "ABORT $RUN: no login session of $TU appeared within 20 s"; wait $H; return; fi'''
assert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new); open('case-body.sh','w').write(s)
PY

### 112. 2026-11-22T04:39:53.035Z -> 2026-11-22T04:39:53.183Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write report renderer that copies listings verbatim"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer && cat > render.py <<'PY'
# ABOUTME: Card #1459: renders the report from a template, replacing {{L|step|run|label}} with that listing block and
# {{S|step|label}} with that login-state block, each copied verbatim from the step transcript.
import re, sys
T = open('transcript.txt', encoding='utf-8').read()
steps = {}
for m in re.[name withheld](r'^### step (\S+) .*?(?=^### step |\Z)', T, re.M | re.S):
    steps[m.group(1)] = m.group(0)
def listing(step, run, label):
    s = steps[step]; out = []
    i = s.find(f'listing run={run} ({label})')
    if i < 0: raise [name withheld](f'missing listing {step} {run} {label}')
    for line in s[i:].split('\n'):
        out.append(line)
        if line.startswith('members recorded='): break
    return '\n'.join(out)
def login(step, label):
    s = steps[step]; i = s.find(f'LOGIN STATE ({label})')
    if i < 0: raise [name withheld](f'missing login {step} {label}')
    j = s.[name withheld]('\n', 0, i) + 1; out = []
    for k, line in enumerate(s[j:].split('\n')):
        if k > 0 and re.match(r'^\d{4}-\d\d-\d\dT', line): break
        if line.startswith('listing run='): break
        out.append(line)
    while out and not out[-1].strip(): out.pop()
    return '\n'.join(out)
src = open(sys.argv[1]).read()
src = re.sub(r'\{\{L\|([^|]+)\|([^|]+)\|([^}]+)\}\}', lambda m: listing(*m.groups()), src)
src = re.sub(r'\{\{S\|([^|]+)\|([^}]+)\}\}', lambda m: login(*m.groups()), src)
open(sys.argv[2], 'w').write(src); print('rendered')
PY
echo ok
--- result ---
ok

#!/bin/sh
# ABOUTME: Git's pre-commit hook. Refuses a commit made with a failing suite.

exec "$(dirname "$0")/refuse-if-suite-red"
#!/bin/sh
# ABOUTME: Reads the remote's default branch and refuses pushes to it, or any push when its name
# is unavailable, before refusing a push made with a failing suite. It skips the suite when the
# tree of every pushed tip, peeled to its commit, is one a green commit or merge hook recorded.

set -u

remote=$1
refuse_unknown() {
  printf 'refused: the default branch for remote %s is unknown; run git remote set-head <remote> -a.\n' "$remote" >&2
  exit 1
}

default_ref=$(git symbolic-ref -q "refs/remotes/$remote/HEAD") || refuse_unknown
prefix="refs/remotes/$remote/"
case $default_ref in
  "$prefix"*) default_branch=${default_ref#"$prefix"} ;;
  *) refuse_unknown ;;
esac

#!/bin/bash
# ABOUTME: Runs a command in the one host-wide test slot, once one-minute load is <= 24 (O46).
# Usage: suite-slot.sh <label> -- <command> [args...]. Lock, log and the first-come-first-served
# queue of waiters live in $SUITE_SLOT_DIR, by default $RIGGER_WORKTREE_ROOT/suite-slot.
# RIGGER_WORKTREE_ROOT defaults to $HOME/GitHub/rigger-worktrees.
# Exits with the command's status (128+N for signal N); 2 usage; 71 cannot record or take the
# lock; 75 load average unreadable after retries; 128+N if the wrapper itself gets signal N.

usage() { echo "usage: suite-slot.sh <label> -- <command> [args...]" >&2; exit 2; }
[ $# -ge 3 ] && [ "$2" = "--" ] && [ -n "$1" ] || usage
LABEL=$1; shift 2

DIR=${SUITE_SLOT_DIR:-${RIGGER_WORKTREE_ROOT:-$HOME/GitHub/rigger-worktrees}/suite-slot}
LOCK=$DIR/lock; LOG=$DIR/suite-slot.log; CAND=$DIR/.cand.$$; RECLAIM=$DIR/reclaim.d
QUEUE=$DIR/queue
MAX=${SUITE_SLOT_MAX_LOAD:-24}; POLL=${SUITE_SLOT_POLL:-5}; TRIES=3
mkdir -p "$DIR" || exit 71

iso() { date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ; }
lstart() { local s; s=$(ps -o lstart= -p "$1" 2>/dev/null) || return 1; echo $s; }
# epoch <lstart text>: that wall-clock time read as UTC, in seconds; fails if it does not parse.
epoch() {
  LC_ALL=C TZ=UTC date -j -f '%a %b %d %T %Y' "$*" +%s 2>/dev/null ||
    LC_ALL=C TZ=UTC date -d "$*" +%s 2>/dev/null
}
# [name withheld] <pid>: the process's start in seconds since the epoch, whatever the host's [name withheld].
[name withheld]() { local s; s=$(LC_ALL=C TZ=UTC ps -o lstart= -p "$1" 2>/dev/null) && [ -n "$s" ] && epoch $s; }
field() { printf '%s\n' "$2" | sed -n "s/^$1=//p"; }
# gone <pid or -pgid>: true only on [name withheld], so "not permitted" or any other failure means present.
gone() { perl -e 'exit(kill(0, $ARGV[0]) ? 1 : $!{[name withheld]} ? 0 : 1)' -- "$1"; }

{
 "file_path": "/Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer/report.[name withheld].md",
 "content": "ABOUTME: Spike findings for card #1459: whether an unprivileged Linux supervisor under WSL2 obtains a delegated cgroup and\nends an agent's whole tree with cgroup.kill when its user has no login session.\n\n# Whole-tree containment with no login session, on Linux under WSL2\n\n## The answer\n\n**With linger set (RL) or a system unit (RS), yes. With neither (R0), no.** The user under test was a dedicated, unprivileged\nWSL user, `rigger-spike`, created for this card by the owner's ruling and deleted at the end.\n\n- **R0, linger unset and nothing changed.** A supervisor that starts with no session open gets no cgroup it can write to.\n  Its cgroup is `/non-systemd`, owned by root. It has no user manager to ask, and the system manager refuses it. N1 and N3\n  are therefore \"not obtained\".\n- **R0, when the last session closes while a tree runs (N2, the engine's own case).** The tree dies, but not by Rigger's\n  hand. Ten seconds after the session closed, logind stopped the user's systemd manager. The manager sent the supervisor\n  `SIGTERM`, and systemd then sent `SIGKILL` to all six members and the fixture root. The cgroup was gone before anything\n  could write `cgroup.kill`.\n- **RL, `loginctl enable-linger` for the user.** The user manager runs with no session. A user unit with `Delegate=yes`\n  gives the supervisor a cgroup whose `cgroup.kill` the user owns. `cgroup.kill` ended every member in N1, N2 and N3. Each\n  time, the cgroup read `populated 0` within 0.35 to 0.91 ms of the write.\n- **RS, a system unit with `User=rigger-spike` and `Delegate=yes`.** The same held, with one difference. The unit's own\n  `cgroup.kill` stays root's and is not writable. The `tree` child cgroup that the supervisor makes is the user's, and\n  writing it ended every member, within 0.27 to 1.27 ms.\n- **Whatever the route, WSL bounds it.** The distro instance stopped 10 to 20 s after its last `wsl.exe` client went away,\n  even with linger set. Nothing a route sets up outlives that.\n\nI recommend RS for an engine that runs unattended under WSL2, with RL as the fallback where no root is available at\ninstall time. Both are under \"Recommendation\", with what would reverse it. This report hands over evidence. It changes\nno code, requirement or recorded decision.\n\n## What ran, and where\n\nEvery command that reached the WSL2 machine or the Windows machine ran through the laptop's step runner\n(`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/source/orch.cjs`). That is a judgment, from these premises: such a process inherits a root-owned cgroup, has no user\nmanager to ask, and cannot use the system manager. A start through `wsl.exe` is not that case. `wsl.exe -u <user>` opens a\nWSL login session for the user, as s2 showed.\n\n**No routes beyond R0, RL and RS were weighed.** A root-run supervisor is outside the card. A user-owned cgroup made by\nroot with `chown` is not one of systemd's delegation routes, and would be its own configuration.\n\n## The fixture, the listings and how a run is counted\n\nThe fixture is #1430's six members, M1 to M6, unchanged in behaviour\n(`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/source/fixture.sh`). Each sleeps 300 s, and M3 and M6 start new sessions. M6\nalso moves to `/`, M4 is reparented, and M5 ignores `SIGTERM`. The fixture root is the supervised command, so a listing\ncounts seven processes: the root and M1 to M6. M4's parent exits at once by design, and shows as `NO`.\n\nThe changes from #1430's fixture: each process that starts a member writes that member's pid and `/proc` starttime, as it\nstarts it, with bash builtins only, into the run's `members.txt`. The supervisor and the fresh writer are new\n(`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/source/supervisor.cjs` and\n`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/source/writer.cjs`).\n\nThe listing (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/source/list.cjs`) reads `/proc` and starts no process. A member is\n`yes` only when its recorded pid exists with the recorded starttime and is not a zombie. Any other live process naming\nthe run is listed as unrecorded, and there was none in any run.\n\n**No live process** means `cgroup.events` reads `populated 0`, or the cgroup is gone, as #1430 read it. The writer polls\n`cgroup.events` in a tight loop for 50 ms, then every 1 ms, for up to 20 s. That 20 s is the bound for \"neither happened\".\n\n**A run counts as measuring `cgroup.kill`** only where the listing taken immediately before the write, by the writing\nprocess itself, shows all seven alive. All six counted runs did. R0's N2 did not reach a write (\"N2 under R0\").\n\n## N1: the supervisor starts with no session, then ends the tree\n\n### RL (rla)\n\nLogin state during the run, with the tree running, before the write:\n\n```\n{{S|rla|rl-n1: tree running, before the cgroup.kill write}}\n```\n\nImmediately before the write, and the write:\n\n```\n{{L|rla|rl-n1|supervisor, immediately before the cgroup.kill write}}\n2026-11-22T03:48:11.890Z cgroup.kill write started 2026-11-22T03:48:11.889Z ; cgroup.events read \"populated 0\" 0.908 ms after the write\n```\n\nAt least 12 s after the write:\n\n```\n{{L|rla|rl-n1|driver: after DONE, at least 12 s after the write}}\n```\n\n### RS (rsa)\n\n```\n{{S|rsa|rs-n1: tree running, before the cgroup.kill write}}\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rsa|rs-n1|supervisor, immediately before the cgroup.kill write}}\n2026-11-22T03:49:12.235Z cgroup.kill write started 2026-11-22T03:49:12.234Z ; cgroup.events read \"populated 0\" 1.266 ms after the write\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rsa|rs-n1|driver: after DONE, at least 12 s after the write}}\n```\n\n## N3: the supervisor killed outright with no session, then a fresh writer\n\nThe driver sent `kill -9` to the supervisor, which it had recorded by pid and starttime. Twelve seconds later it listed\nthe orphaned cgroup. A fresh process then wrote `cgroup.kill`: `writer.cjs`, started by the driver as `rigger-spike`,\nholding nothing from the supervisor. It found the cgroup by the path the supervisor had logged. Each unit ran with\n`KillMode=process`, so systemd sent no signal to the rest of the unit when the supervisor died.\n\n### RL (rla)\n\n```\n{{S|rla|rl-n3: tree running, before the kill}}\n```\n\n```\n2026-11-22T03:48:28.443029Z $ kill -9 3151\n2026-11-22T03:48:28.443478Z kill -9 sent to recorded supervisor 3151\n```\n\nThe members, alive in the orphaned cgroup before the fresh write:\n\n```\n{{L|rla|rl-n3|driver: 12 s after kill -9 of the supervisor, before the fresh write}}\n```\n\n```\n{{S|rla|rl-n3: supervisor killed, before the fresh write}}\n```\n\nThe fresh writer's own listing immediately before its write, and the write:\n\n```\n{{L|rla|rl-n3|fresh writer, immediately before the cgroup.kill write}}\n2026-11-22T03:48:40.501Z cgroup.kill write started 2026-11-22T03:48:40.501Z ; cgroup.events read \"populated 0\" 0.351 ms after the write\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rla|rl-n3|driver: 12 s after the fresh write}}\n```\n\n### RS (rsa)\n\n```\n{{S|rsa|rs-n3: tree running, before the kill}}\n```\n\n```\n2026-11-22T03:49:28.912705Z $ kill -9 4280\n2026-11-22T03:49:28.913154Z kill -9 sent to recorded supervisor 4280\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rsa|rs-n3|driver: 12 s after kill -9 of the supervisor, before the fresh write}}\n```\n\n```\n{{S|rsa|rs-n3: supervisor killed, before the fresh write}}\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rsa|rs-n3|fresh writer, immediately before the cgroup.kill write}}\n2026-11-22T03:49:40.964Z cgroup.kill write started 2026-11-22T03:49:40.964Z ; cgroup.events read \"populated 0\" 0.619 ms after the write\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rsa|rs-n3|driver: 12 s after the fresh write}}\n```\n\n## N2: the supervisor starts under a session, then every session closes\n\nEach N2 run went the same way:\n\n1. The driver opened a `rigger-spike` login through WSL interop.\n2. It started the supervisor under that session and waited for the supervisor's ready line.\n3. It waited for the login command to exit, and then ran `loginctl terminate-session` on the session left open.\n4. It recorded member liveness every second for 30 s, and read the login state.\n5. It listed the members, and then let the supervisor write.\n\n### N2 under R0 (r0c): the user manager ends the tree first\n\nThe session, open before the supervisor started:\n\n```\n{{S|r0c|r0-n2: session open, before supervisor start}}\n```\n\nClosing it:\n\n```\n2026-11-22T04:20:09.641941Z interop login command exited (0); sessions of rigger-spike: c4 \n2026-11-22T04:20:11.648250Z $ loginctl terminate-session c4   (its leader is the spike's own login, recorded above)\nexit 0\n2026-11-22T04:20:11.659039Z every session of rigger-spike closed at 2026-11-22T04:20:11.656508Z (tu_sessions empty: yes)\n```\n\nMember liveness each second after the close (an excerpt; all 30 lines are in step r0c):\n\n```\n2026-11-22T04:20:20.932627Z t+9s after close: members alive=7 supervisor alive user@1001 active\n2026-11-22T04:20:21.956036Z t+10s after close: members alive=7 supervisor gone user@1001 deactivating\n2026-11-22T04:20:22.988328Z t+11s after close: members alive=0 supervisor gone user@1001 inactive\n```\n\nThe login state and the listing, 30 s after the close and before any write:\n\n```\n{{S|r0c|r0-n2: 30 s after every session closed, before the cgroup.kill write}}\n```\n\n```\n{{L|r0c|r0-n2|driver: 30 s after every session closed, before the cgroup.kill write}}\n```\n\n**What ended the members:** the user manager's stop, not `cgroup.kill`. The journal read in the same step shows the\nsequence:\n\n```\n2026-11-22T04:20:11.654139+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd-logind[213]: Removed session c4.\n2026-11-22T04:20:21.907868+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1]: Stopping user@1001.service - User Manager for UID 1001...\n2026-11-22T04:20:21.915637+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1121]: fx1459-r0-n2.service: Main process exited, code=exited, status=143/n/a\n2026-11-22T04:20:21.915735+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1121]: fx1459-r0-n2.service: Unit process 1428 (bash) remains running after unit stopped.\n2026-11-22T04:20:22.007379+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1]: user@1001.service: Killing process 1428 (bash) with signal SIGKILL.\n2026-11-22T04:20:22.007887+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1]: user@1001.service: Killing process 1436 (sleep) with signal SIGKILL.\n2026-11-22T04:20:22.010540+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1]: Stopped user@1001.service - User Manager for UID 1001.\n```\n\nThe supervisor logged `2026-11-22T04:20:21.912Z supervisor received SIGTERM; exiting 143`. The journal lists all seven\nfixture processes, 1428, 1430, 1431, 1433, 1434, 1435 and 1436, as remaining and then killed with `SIGKILL`.\n\nFrom the session's removal (04:20:11.654139Z) to `Stopping user@1001.service` (04:20:21.907868Z) is 10.25 s. That is a\nmeasurement: both endpoints are journal lines printed in step r0c. It matches systemd's default `UserStopDelaySec` of\n10 s, which is a judgment, since this host's value was not printed.\n\n**No write was made.** By the time the driver came to write, the supervisor was gone and so was the cgroup. The driver\nprinted `tree cgroup /sys/fs/cgroup/user.slice/user-1001.slice/user@1001.service/app.slice/fx1459-r0-n2.service/tree does not exist`,\nfollowed by its `ls -ld` failing. So this run does not measure `cgroup.kill`, and neither `populated 0` nor the cgroup's\nremoval followed a write. The listing at least 12 s later:\n\n```\n{{L|r0c|r0-n2|driver: at least 12 s after the ending}}\n```\n\n### N2 under RL (rlc)\n\n```\n{{S|rlc|rl-n2: session open, before supervisor start}}\n```\n\n```\n2026-11-22T04:36:14.146092Z $ loginctl terminate-session c5   (its leader is the spike's own login, recorded above)\n2026-11-22T04:36:14.155904Z every session of rigger-spike closed at 2026-11-22T04:36:14.153580Z (tu_sessions empty: yes)\n```\n\nAll seven stayed alive through the 30 s, and the user manager stayed `active`, since linger keeps it.\n\n```\n{{S|rlc|rl-n2: 30 s after every session closed, before the cgroup.kill write}}\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rlc|rl-n2|driver: 30 s after every session closed, before the cgroup.kill write}}\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rlc|rl-n2|supervisor, immediately before the cgroup.kill write}}\n2026-11-22T04:36:45.103Z cgroup.kill write started 2026-11-22T04:36:45.102Z ; cgroup.events read \"populated 0\" 0.576 ms after the write\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rlc|rl-n2|driver: after DONE, at least 12 s after the write}}\n```\n\n### N2 under RS (rsc)\n\n```\n{{S|rsc|rs-n2: session open, before supervisor start}}\n```\n\n```\n2026-11-22T04:37:50.657285Z $ loginctl terminate-session c4   (its leader is the spike's own login, recorded above)\n2026-11-22T04:37:50.666788Z every session of rigger-spike closed at 2026-11-22T04:37:50.664161Z (tu_sessions empty: yes)\n```\n\nThe user manager stopped about 10 s after the close (`user@1001 inactive` from t+10s). The tree, in a system unit, was\nuntouched: all seven were alive at every second.\n\n```\n{{S|rsc|rs-n2: 30 s after every session closed, before the cgroup.kill write}}\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rsc|rs-n2|driver: 30 s after every session closed, before the cgroup.kill write}}\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rsc|rs-n2|supervisor, immediately before the cgroup.kill write}}\n2026-11-22T04:38:21.760Z cgroup.kill write started 2026-11-22T04:38:21.760Z ; cgroup.events read \"populated 0\" 0.272 ms after the write\n```\n\n```\n{{L|rsc|rs-n2|driver: after DONE, at least 12 s after the write}}\n```\n\n## The table\n\nEach cell gives the state of M1 to M6 and the fixture root at least 10 s after the ending, and the step whose listing\nshows it.\n\n| Route | N1 | N2 | N3 |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| R0 | not obtained (r0b) | all ended before any write, by the user manager's stop 10.25 s after the session closed; no `cgroup.kill` (r0c) | not obtained (r0b) |\n| RL | all ended by `cgroup.kill`; `populated 0` after 0.908 ms (rla) | all ended by `cgroup.kill`; 0.576 ms (rlc) | all alive in the orphan 12 s after `kill -9`; all ended by the fresh writer; 0.351 ms (rla) |\n| RS | all ended by `cgroup.kill`; 1.266 ms (rsa) | all ended by `cgroup.kill`; 0.272 ms (rsc) | all alive in the orphan 12 s after `kill -9`; all ended by the fresh writer; 0.619 ms (rsa) |\n\nEvery time is a measurement. Its two endpoints are the write and the first `cgroup.events` read of `populated 0`. Both are\ntaken by `process.hrtime` in the one process that wrote, supervisor or fresh writer, and printed in the step named.\n\n## Whether WSL kept running\n\nEach driver printed the VM's boot id and the distro's pid 1 starttime when it started, at each case's start and end, and\nat its end. Within every step, both stayed the same through every interval in which `rigger-spike` had no session:\n\n| Step | Boot id | Distro pid 1 starttime | From | To |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| r0b | 93e1a4db-e2d2-416b-b58d-7145914f1de6 | 12689 | 03:48:00.097785Z | 03:48:01.837474Z |\n| rla | 93e1a4db-e2d2-416b-b58d-7145914f1de6 | 12689 | 03:48:08.155820Z | 03:48:54.257785Z |\n| rsa | 93e1a4db-e2d2-416b-b58d-7145914f1de6 | 12689 | 03:49:08.759698Z | 03:49:54.702499Z |\n| r0c | 3c4522f0-1741-42f8-9d01-88ef813a8399 | 64 | 04:20:01.286844Z | 04:20:58.070473Z |\n| rlc | b7aea7c3-a116-46b4-9610-75a534a80142 | 58 | 04:36:03.754767Z | 04:37:00.165060Z |\n| rsc | b7aea7c3-a116-46b4-9610-75a534a80142 | 11117 | 04:37:40.180651Z | 04:38:38.131921Z |\n\nThe driver's own `wsl.exe` connection held the distro up throughout. Between steps it did not stay up:\n\n- After s1 returned at 03:05:51Z, p2 at 03:06:12Z found new pids for the user manager and `sshd` in the same VM boot. The\n  distro had restarted.\n- rlc ended at 04:37:00Z. By 04:37:21Z (step w9-1), `wsl.exe -l -v` read `Stopped`. rsc then found a new distro pid 1\n  starttime (11117 against 58).\n\n**Finding:** inside WSL2, the distro stops roughly 10 to 20 s after its last `wsl.exe` client exits. That is a judgment,\nfrom these premises: three observed gaps, each bounded by step times; linger set for `[name withheld]`; [name withheld]'s SSH session open.\nNeither linger nor an SSH login keeps it up, and every route's tree ends with it. Why WSL stops while idle is outside this\ncard.\n\n## What the WSL2 results leave unproved about a native Linux host\n\n- **The distro's own lifetime.** On native Linux nothing stops the OS 10 to 20 s after the last client. RL and RS would\n  keep a tree as long as the host runs, which was not measured here.\n- **How a session opens and closes.** WSL opens a login session per user with `login -- <user>` and does not close it when\n  the command ends. N2 closed it with `terminate-session`. A native SSH or console logout closes its session itself, which\n  was not measured.\n- **Starts with no session.** R0's supervisor was started by `setpriv` from root's `/non-systemd` shell. On native Linux a\n  start from [name withheld] or another system service lands in that service's cgroup. That is also root's, but it was not\n  measured.\n- **Policy.** polkit refused the system manager to an unprivileged user here. A host with a different polkit policy could\n  differ.\n- **logind's settings.** `KillUserProcesses` was `false` and `UserStopDelaySec` was the default. A distribution that sets\n  `KillUserProcesses=yes` would also kill session-scope processes at logout. That does not change RL's or RS's units,\n  but it was not measured.\n- **Kernel and systemd.** Only kernel 6.18.58.1-microsoft-standard-WSL2 and systemd 259 were measured.\n\n## Recommendation\n\n**For whole-tree containment with no login session, Rigger can rely on RS, or on RL.** It cannot rely on R0.\n\nThe next card should do these things:\n\n- **RS first.** Install one system unit, a template, that runs the engine's supervisor as the unprivileged user with\n  `Delegate=yes` and `KillMode=process`. Make each dispatch's cgroup a child of the unit's cgroup, and end it by writing\n  the child's `cgroup.kill`. The unit's own `cgroup.kill` is root's and must not be the one relied on (rsa). This needs\n  root once, at install, and nothing at run time.\n- **RL where no root is available at run time but an administrator can enable linger once.** Start the supervisor through\n  the user manager (`systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes`, or a user unit). Treat linger as a precondition the engine\n  checks at start: `loginctl show-user <user> -p Linger` reads `Linger=yes`.\n- **Either way,** record each dispatch's cgroup path durably, so that the next start can write `cgroup.kill` to an orphan.\n  N3 showed that works under both routes with no session open. It is the cgroup analogue of `R-STATE-10`.\n- **Refuse R0 for unattended work.** A supervisor with neither linger nor a system unit has no writable cgroup when no\n  session is open. If it started under a session, the user manager's stop ends its whole tree, supervisor included,\n  about 10 s after the last session closes (r0c). The engine should detect this and refuse, rather than run without\n  containment.\n- **Under WSL2, say plainly that a dispatch lives only as long as some `wsl.exe` client keeps the distro up.**\n\nThe next card must not assume these things:\n\n- That a WSL login session closes when the [name withheld] command ends (s2).\n- That systemd's user-manager stop is harmless to a tree started under a session without linger. It sent `SIGTERM` and\n  then `SIGKILL` to everything in the unit (r0c).\n- That the unit's own cgroup is writable under RS (rsa).\n- That repeated WSL logins of one user in one distro instance each open a session. After a `terminate-session`, the next\n  two did not (rlb, rsb, under \"What failed\").\n\n**What would reverse the recommendation:**\n\n- A native Linux host where a system unit's `Delegate=yes` child cgroup is not writable by its `User=`, or where linger\n  does not keep the user manager running with no session.\n- A distribution or policy that stops lingering user managers, or kills units, on the last logout.\n- A target environment that forbids installing a system unit and enabling linger alike. Rigger would then have no\n  whole-tree containment with no session, and the fallback is today's process-group route, with its gap at M6.\n- Evidence that WSL2 can keep a distro up with no `wsl.exe` client. That would make WSL a fit host for long unattended\n  runs, which this report says it is not.\n\n## What this would change, as a proposal\n\nThis section is a proposal for the PM, the architect and the owner. The report changes none of these.\n\n| Document | What the recommendation would change |\n|---|---|\n| `D13` | It makes macOS v0's only host. A Linux host's containment backend would need either a system unit or linger as an install precondition |\n| `R-STATE-17` | It defines containment as a process group plus a census of the dispatch's directory. A delegated cgroup child per dispatch would also hold M6 |\n| `R-STATE-9`, `R-STATE-10` and `R-STATE-11` | On the killed-outright path, the next start ends each recorded group. On Linux the recorded thing would be a cgroup path, which N3 showed reachable by a fresh process with no session |\n| `ARCHITECTURE.md`, \"Failure model\" | L0's containment paragraph, and what the engine checks at start: linger or a system unit on Linux |\n\n## What failed\n\n| Step | What happened | Why | Full output |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| r0a | The driver stopped after its first records, and no case ran | The interop `powershell.exe` that `winrec` starts read the rest of the script from `bash -s`'s stdin. Every later driver runs its body as a function, with stdin from `/dev/null` | `docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/failed-runs/r0a.txt` |\n| rlb | N2 under RL: no `rigger-spike` login session appeared, so the supervisor never started under one. The run went on and wrote `cgroup.kill` (0.412 ms), but it is not an N2 run and is not counted | It followed r0c in the same distro instance, where the spike had ended WSL's login session for that user with `terminate-session`. Why WSL then opened no session is not known. Later drivers abort N2 when no session appears | `docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/failed-runs/rlb.txt` |\n| rsb | N2 under RS: the same as rlb (1.720 ms, not counted) | The same | `docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/failed-runs/rsb.txt` |\n| s3, f2, f1e, c1 | Not failures. s3 deleted the user before the reruns, so s4 recreated it ([1459#7106924301](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1459#issuecomment-7106924301)), and f2, f1e and c1 were superseded by f2b, f1f and c2 | — | in the transcript |\n| f1, f1b, f1c, f1d | Four attempts at the final Windows listing each hung, and `ssh` gave up after 2 minutes with `Timeout, server 10.0.0.29 not responding.` | Not root-caused. A small script on the same route worked (w6, w7). Each attempt fed PowerShell the record's 153 pid and time pairs on stdin; f1e, without them, worked, and the comparison moved to the laptop. Each attempt left processes running on Windows (\"Processes still running that the record does not hold\") | `docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/failed-runs/f1.txt`, `f1b.txt`, `f1c.txt`, `f1d.txt` in the same directory |\n| s1 | Its process scan recorded [name withheld]'s SSH login and `[name withheld]`'s user-manager processes as the spike's | The scan recorded every new process in `user.slice`. The eight lines are voided in the record with a dated note, not deleted, and the scan was narrowed before s2 | in the transcript, step s1 |\n\n## Custody\n\n### The transcript\n\n`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/transcript.txt` is the step runner's transcript. Every command run on the WSL2\nmachine and the Windows machine, and each laptop step run through it, is there. Each entry carries its exact argv, its\nstdin verbatim with a sha256, its UTC start and end, the host and the full output. Inside a WSL step, each line the script\nprinted carries its own UTC time.\n\n`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/session-log.txt` holds every tool call in the spike engineer's session record:\nthe laptop commands outside the runner, the report's writing, the checks, the commits and the push. It was generated by\n#1430's `gen-session-log.py`, unchanged except for its input and output paths and its header. Its scope is stated under\n\"Scope of the record\", below.\n\n### The credential scan\n\n{{SCAN}}\n\n### Files written outside the spike's own directories\n\n| Host | File | Reason | Removed |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| WSL2 | `/etc/passwd`, `/etc/shadow`, `/etc/group`, `/etc/gshadow`, `/etc/subuid` and `/etc/subgid`: one `rigger-spike` line each, written twice (s2, s4) | the user under test | Yes. After s5, line counts were back to 28, 28, 57, 57, 1 and 1, and `grep -c rigger-spike` printed 0 for each |\n| WSL2 | `/home/rigger-spike/`, from `/etc/skel`, holding the spike's directory | the user's home | Yes, by `userdel -r` (s3, s5). `ls -ld /home/rigger-spike` printed `No such file or directory` |\n| WSL2 | `/var/lib/systemd/linger/rigger-spike` | RL | Yes, by `loginctl disable-linger rigger-spike` at the end of rla and rlc. s5's `ls -la /var/lib/systemd/linger` lists only `[name withheld]` |\n| WSL2 | `/etc/systemd/system/fx1459-rs@.service` | RS | Yes. rsa and rsc printed `removed '/etc/systemd/system/fx1459-rs@.service'` and `No files found for fx1459-rs@x.service.` |\n| WSL2 | `/run/user/1001/` and its transient unit files | the user manager's runtime | Yes. s5's `ls` printed `No such file or directory` |\n| WSL2 | `/home/[name withheld]/1459-spike-engineer/`, the spike's first directory | s1, before the user existed | Yes. It moved into the user's home in s2. s5's `ls` printed `No such file or directory` |\n| WSL2 | the system and user journals' entries for the units and sessions | systemd logs them | No. Removing them means editing the host's journal, so they are left, as #1430 left them |\n| WSL2 | cgroups under `user-1001.slice` and `system-fx1459\\x2drs.slice` | kernel state, not files | Yes. f2b's `ls -d` printed `No such file or directory` for both |\n| Windows | none | — | — |\n| Laptop | none outside the worktree, its gitignored spike directory (spikes/1459-spike-engineer) and the harness's own task-output files | — | — |\n\n### Host-configuration changes\n\nThe stated intent went up before the first change, at\n[1459#7073278550](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1459#issuecomment-7073278550). ### 121. 2026-11-22T04:44:41.348Z -> 2026-11-22T04:44:41.538Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Start writing the report template via shell"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer && cat > report.[name withheld] <<'[name withheld]_END'
ABOUTME: Spike findings for card #1459: whether an unprivileged Linux supervisor under WSL2 obtains a delegated cgroup and
ends an agent's whole tree with cgroup.kill when its user has no login session.

# Whole-tree containment with no login session, on Linux under WSL2

## The answer

**With linger set (RL) or a system unit (RS), yes. With neither (R0), no.** The user under test was a dedicated, unprivileged
WSL user, `rigger-spike`, created for this card under the owner's ruling and deleted at the end.

- **R0, linger unset and nothing changed.** A supervisor that starts with no session open gets no cgroup it can write to.
  Its cgroup is `/non-systemd`, owned by root. It has no user manager to ask, and the system manager refuses it. N1 and N3
  are therefore "not obtained".
- **R0, when the last session closes while a tree runs (N2, the engine's own case).** The tree dies, but not by Rigger's
  hand. Ten seconds after the session closed, logind stopped the user's systemd manager. The manager sent the supervisor
  `SIGTERM`, and systemd then sent `SIGKILL` to the fixture root and all six members. The cgroup was gone before anything
  could write `cgroup.kill`.
- **RL, `loginctl enable-linger` for the user.** The user manager runs with no session. A user unit with `Delegate=yes`
  gives the supervisor a cgroup whose `cgroup.kill` the user owns. `cgroup.kill` ended every member in N1, N2 and N3. Each
  time, the cgroup read `populated 0` within 0.35 to 0.91 ms of the write.
- **RS, a system unit with `User=rigger-spike` and `Delegate=yes`.** The same held, with one difference. The unit's own
  `cgroup.kill` stays root's and is not writable. The `tree` child cgroup that the supervisor makes is the user's, and
  writing it ended every member, within 0.27 to 1.27 ms.
- **Whatever the route, WSL bounds it.** The distro instance stopped 10 to 20 s after its last `wsl.exe` client went away,
  even with linger set. Nothing a route sets up outlives that.

I recommend RS for an engine that runs unattended under WSL2, with RL as the fallback where no root is available at
install time. Both are under "Recommendation", with what would reverse it. This report hands over evidence. It changes
no code, requirement or recorded decision.
[name withheld]_END
echo ok
--- result ---

## The user under test, and how the spike reached WSL

**Why not `[name withheld]`.** p2 found that [name withheld], on the owner's laptop, logs into WSL as `[name withheld]` over SSH at every distro start.
That holds a `Class=user` session for `[name withheld]` whenever WSL runs. `[name withheld]` also had linger set before the spike:
`[name withheld]` has mtime 2026-11-05 01:11:20Z. The owner ruled that the spike may create one dedicated user
instead, at [1459#7106921307](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1459#issuecomment-7106921307). It was named before
it was created, at [1459#7106922830](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1459#issuecomment-7106922830). `[name withheld]`, its
sessions, its linger and [name withheld] were not touched.

**How the spike's own access affected sessions.** Each `wsl.exe -u root` connection opened sessions for root and for no
one else. p0b listed them:

```
SESSION  UID USER SEAT LEADER CLASS [name withheld] IDLE SINCE
     c1 1000 [name withheld] -    310    manager       -     no   -
     c2    0 root -    422    user-early    pts/1 no   -
     c3    0 root -    465    manager-early -     no   -
```

- `c1` is `[name withheld]`'s user manager, there because of `[name withheld]`'s linger.
- `c2` is WSL's `login -- root` for the spike's connection.
- `c3` is root's user manager.

The spike opened a `rigger-spike` session only where a case needed one: the s2 probe, and N2's interop login
(`wsl.exe -u rigger-spike -e sleep 8`). Every N1 and N3 login-state reading below shows no `rigger-spike` session. The
other `[name withheld]` sessions that come and go in the listings, such as `c4` and `c6`, are [name withheld]'s.

**A WSL login session does not close when its command ends.** In s2, the interop login opened session `c4`
(`Class=user`, on `pts/3`). The `wsl.exe` command exited at t+6 s, and `c4` was still listed at t+30 s:

```
2026-11-22T03:46:00.849380Z t+6s holder exited sessions: c4(user,leader 1330) c5(manager,leader 1361)  user@1001: active
2026-11-22T03:46:26.876777Z t+30s holder exited sessions: c4(user,leader 1330) c5(manager,leader 1361)  user@1001: active
```

So in N2, closing every session meant running `loginctl terminate-session` on the session the spike's own login had
opened.

## Did the supervisor obtain a writable cgroup

Each row quotes the supervisor's `/proc/self/cgroup` and `ls -l` of `cgroup.kill`, from its log in the step named.

| Route | Session open | `/proc/self/cgroup` of the supervisor | Its cgroup's `cgroup.kill` | The child `tree/cgroup.kill` | Obtained |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R0 (r0b, run r0-n1) | no | `0::/non-systemd` | `--w------- 1 root root 0 Nov 21 21:48 /sys/fs/cgroup/non-systemd/cgroup.kill` | not made: `mkdir /sys/fs/cgroup/non-systemd/tree : EACCES` | **no** |
| R0 (r0c, run r0-n2) | yes | `0::/user.slice/user-1001.slice/user@1001.service/app.slice/fx1459-r0-n2.service` | `--w------- 1 rigger-spike rigger-spike 0 Nov 21 22:20 …/fx1459-r0-n2.service/cgroup.kill` | `--w------- 1 rigger-spike rigger-spike 0 Nov 21 22:20 …/fx1459-r0-n2.service/tree/cgroup.kill` | yes, while the session lasted |
| RL (rla, run rl-n1) | no | `0::/user.slice/user-1001.slice/user@1001.service/app.slice/fx1459-rl-n1.service` | `--w------- 1 rigger-spike rigger-spike 0 Nov 21 21:48 …/fx1459-rl-n1.service/cgroup.kill` | `--w------- 1 rigger-spike rigger-spike 0 Nov 21 21:48 …/fx1459-rl-n1.service/tree/cgroup.kill` | **yes** |
| RS (rsa, run rs-n1) | no | `0::/system.slice/system-fx1459\x2drs.slice/fx1459-rs@n1.service` | `--w------- 1 root         root         0 Nov 21 21:49 …/fx1459-rs@n1.service/cgroup.kill`, with `access(W_OK) on own cgroup.kill: EACCES` | `--w------- 1 rigger-spike rigger-spike 0 Nov 21 21:49 …/fx1459-rs@n1.service/tree/cgroup.kill` | **yes**, through the child |

In each row, `…` stands for `/sys/fs/cgroup` followed by the cgroup path in the same row, which the supervisor's log
prints in full. The dates `ls -l` printed are WSL's local time. Every other run read the same for its route, in its own
supervisor log in the transcript.

```

Run r0-n3, in the same step, printed the same lines with its own pids and times. **N1 and N3 under R0 are "not
obtained".**

This R0 supervisor stands for an engine that something inside Linux starts as its user with no session, such as [name withheld] or
a root-run `setpriv`. That is a judgment, from three premises: such a process inherits a cgroup root owns, it has no user
manager to ask, and polkit refuses it the system manager. A start through `wsl.exe` is not that case, because
`wsl.exe -u <user>` opens a WSL login session for the user, as s2 showed.

**No routes beyond R0, RL and RS were weighed.** A supervisor running as root is outside the card. A cgroup root makes and
hands to the user with `chown` is not one of systemd's delegation routes; it would be a configuration of its own.

## The fixture, the listings, and when a run counts

The fixture is #1430's six members, M1 to M6, with the same behaviour
(`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/source/fixture.sh`). Each sleeps 300 s. M3 and M6 start new sessions, and M6
also moves to `/`. M4 is reparented, and M5 ignores `SIGTERM`. The fixture root is the supervised command, so a listing
counts seven processes: the root and M1 to M6. M4's parent exits at once by design, and shows as `NO`.

Two things changed from #1430:

- Each process that starts a member writes that member's pid and `/proc` starttime into the run's `members.txt`, as it
  starts it, using bash builtins only.
- The supervisor and the fresh writer are new: `docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/source/supervisor.cjs` and
  `docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/source/writer.cjs`.

The listing, `docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/source/list.cjs`, reads `/proc` and starts no process. A member
shows `yes` only when its recorded pid exists with its recorded starttime and is not a zombie. Any other live process
naming the run would be listed as unrecorded, and no run had one.

**No live process** means `cgroup.events` reads `populated 0`, or the cgroup is gone, as #1430 read it. The writer polls
`cgroup.events` in a tight loop for 50 ms, then every 1 ms, for up to 20 s. That 20 s is the bound for "neither happened".

**A run counts as measuring `cgroup.kill`** only where all seven were alive in the listing the writing process took
immediately before its write. All six counted runs had all seven alive. R0's N2 never reached a write (see "N2 under R0").
[name withheld]_END
echo ok
--- result ---
I recommend RS for an engine that runs unattended under WSL2, with RL as the fallback where a system unit cannot be installed but linger can be
set. Both are under "Recommendation", with what would reverse it. This report hands over evidence. It changes
no code, requirement or recorded decision.
ok

## N1: the supervisor starts with no session, then ends the tree

### RL (rla)

## N3: the supervisor killed outright with no session, then a fresh writer

The driver sent `kill -9` to the supervisor, which it had recorded by pid and starttime. Twelve seconds later it listed
the orphaned cgroup. A fresh process then wrote `cgroup.kill`. That process was `writer.cjs`, started by the driver as
`rigger-spike`; it held nothing of the supervisor's, and found the cgroup by the path the supervisor had logged. Each unit
ran with `KillMode=process`, so systemd sent no signal to the rest of the unit when the supervisor died.

## N2: the supervisor starts under a session, then every session closes

Each N2 run took the same steps:

1. The driver opened a `rigger-spike` login through WSL interop.
2. It started the supervisor under that session and waited for the supervisor's ready line.
3. It waited for the login command to exit, then ran `loginctl terminate-session` on the session left open.
4. For 30 s, it recorded every second whether the members were alive.
5. It read the login state and listed the members.
6. It let the supervisor write `cgroup.kill`, or a fresh writer if the supervisor was gone.

### N2 under R0 (r0c): the user manager ends the tree first

The session, open before the supervisor started:

```
{{S|r0c|r0-n2: session open, before supervisor start}}
```

Closing it:

```
2026-11-22T04:20:09.641941Z interop login command exited (0); sessions of rigger-spike: c4 
2026-11-22T04:20:11.648250Z $ loginctl terminate-session c4   (its leader is the spike's own login, recorded above)
exit 0
2026-11-22T04:20:11.659039Z every session of rigger-spike closed at 2026-11-22T04:20:11.656508Z (tu_sessions empty: yes)

**What ended the members was the user manager's stop, not `cgroup.kill`.** The journal read in the same step shows the
sequence. These lines are an excerpt of it:

```
2026-11-22T04:20:11.654139+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd-logind[213]: Removed session c4.
2026-11-22T04:20:21.907868+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1]: Stopping user@1001.service - User Manager for UID 1001...
2026-11-22T04:20:21.915637+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1121]: fx1459-r0-n2.service: Main process exited, code=exited, status=143/n/a
2026-11-22T04:20:21.915735+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1121]: fx1459-r0-n2.service: Unit process 1428 (bash) remains running after unit stopped.
2026-11-22T04:20:22.007379+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1]: user@1001.service: Killing process 1428 (bash) with signal SIGKILL.
2026-11-22T04:20:22.007887+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1]: user@1001.service: Killing process 1436 (sleep) with signal SIGKILL.
2026-11-22T04:20:22.010540+00:00 Host-PC10 systemd[1]: Stopped user@1001.service - User Manager for UID 1001.
```

The supervisor logged `2026-11-22T04:20:21.912Z supervisor received SIGTERM; exiting 143`. The journal names all seven
fixture processes, 1428, 1430, 1431, 1433, 1434, 1435 and 1436, first as remaining and then as killed with `SIGKILL`.

From the session's removal (04:20:11.654139Z) to `Stopping user@1001.service` (04:20:21.907868Z) is 10.25 s. That is a
measurement: both endpoints are journal lines printed in step r0c. That it matches systemd's default `UserStopDelaySec` of
10 s is a judgment, since this host's value was not printed.

**No write was made.** By the time the driver came to write, the supervisor and the cgroup were both gone. The driver
printed
`tree cgroup /sys/fs/cgroup/user.slice/user-1001.slice/user@1001.service/app.slice/fx1459-r0-n2.service/tree does not exist`,
and its `ls -ld` failed. So this run does not measure `cgroup.kill`. The cgroup went at the user manager's stop, not after
any write. The listing at least 12 s later:

```
{{L|r0c|r0-n2|driver: at least 12 s after the ending}}

## The table

Each cell gives the state of the fixture root and M1 to M6 at least 10 s after the ending, and the step whose listing
shows it.

| Route | N1 | N2 | N3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| R0 | not obtained (r0b) | all ended before any write, by the user manager's stop 10.25 s after the session closed; no `cgroup.kill` written (r0c) | not obtained (r0b) |
| RL | all ended by `cgroup.kill`; `populated 0` 0.908 ms after the write (rla) | all ended by `cgroup.kill`; 0.576 ms (rlc) | all alive in the orphan 12 s after `kill -9`; all ended by the fresh writer; 0.351 ms (rla) |
| RS | all ended by `cgroup.kill`; 1.266 ms (rsa) | all ended by `cgroup.kill`; 0.272 ms (rsc) | all alive in the orphan 12 s after `kill -9`; all ended by the fresh writer; 0.619 ms (rsa) |

Every time in the table is a measurement. Its two endpoints are the write and the first `cgroup.events` read of
`populated 0`. The one process that wrote, supervisor or fresh writer, took both with `process.hrtime` and printed them
in the step named.

## Whether WSL kept running

Each driver printed the VM's boot id and the distro's pid 1 starttime when it started, at each case's start and end, and
at its end. Within each step both stayed the same, through every interval in which `rigger-spike` had no session:

| Step | Boot id | Distro pid 1 starttime | First reading | Last reading |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| r0b | 93e1a4db-e2d2-416b-b58d-7145914f1de6 | 12689 | 03:48:00.097785Z | 03:48:01.837474Z |
| rla | 93e1a4db-e2d2-416b-b58d-7145914f1de6 | 12689 | 03:48:08.155820Z | 03:48:54.257785Z |
| rsa | 93e1a4db-e2d2-416b-b58d-7145914f1de6 | 12689 | 03:49:08.759698Z | 03:49:54.702499Z |
| r0c | 3c4522f0-1741-42f8-9d01-88ef813a8399 | 64 | 04:20:01.286844Z | 04:20:58.070473Z |
| rlc | b7aea7c3-a116-46b4-9610-75a534a80142 | 58 | 04:36:03.754767Z | 04:37:00.165060Z |
| rsc | b7aea7c3-a116-46b4-9610-75a534a80142 | 11117 | 04:37:40.180651Z | 04:38:38.131921Z |

The driver's own `wsl.exe` connection held the distro up throughout. Between steps it did not stay up:

- After s1 returned at 03:05:51Z, p2 at 03:06:12Z found new pids for the user manager and `sshd` in the same VM boot, so
  the distro had restarted.
- rlc ended at 04:37:00Z. At 04:37:21Z (step w9-1), `wsl.exe -l -v` read `Stopped`, and rsc then found a new distro pid 1
  starttime, 11117 against 58.

**Finding:** inside WSL2, the distro stops roughly 10 to 20 s after its last `wsl.exe` client exits. That is a judgment,
from three observed gaps, each bounded by step times. Linger was set for `[name withheld]` and [name withheld]'s SSH session was open
throughout, so neither keeps the distro up, and every route's tree ends with it. Why WSL stops while idle is outside this
card.

## What the WSL2 results leave unproved about a native Linux host

- **How long the OS runs.** On native Linux nothing stops the OS 10 to 20 s after its last client. RL and RS would keep a
  tree as long as the host runs, and that was not measured here.
- **How a session opens and closes.** WSL opens a login session per user with `login -- <user>`, and does not close it
  when the command ends; N2 closed it with `terminate-session`. A native SSH or console logout closes its own session,
  and that was not measured.
- **Starts with no session.** R0's supervisor was started by `setpriv` from root's `/non-systemd` shell. On native Linux, a
  start from [name withheld] or another system service lands in that service's cgroup. That cgroup is also root's, but it was not
  measured.
- **Policy.** polkit refused the system manager to an unprivileged user here. A host with a different polkit policy could
  behave differently.
- **logind's settings.** `KillUserProcesses` was `false` and `UserStopDelaySec` was the default. A distribution that sets
  `KillUserProcesses=yes` would also kill session-scope processes at logout. That does not reach RL's or RS's units, but
  it was not measured.
- **Kernel and systemd.** Only kernel 6.18.58.1-microsoft-standard-WSL2 and systemd 259 were measured.
[name withheld]_END
echo ok

### 124. 2026-11-22T04:46:56.780Z -> 2026-11-22T04:46:56.965Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Append recommendation, failures and custody sections"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer && cat >> report.[name withheld] <<'[name withheld]_END'

## Recommendation

**For whole-tree containment with no login session, Rigger can rely on RS or on RL. It cannot rely on R0.**

The next card should do these things:

- **Prefer RS.** Install one system unit, a template, that runs the engine's supervisor as the unprivileged user with
  `Delegate=yes` and `KillMode=process`. Give each dispatch a child cgroup of the unit's cgroup, and end the dispatch by
  writing the child's `cgroup.kill`. Do not rely on the unit's own `cgroup.kill`, which stays root's (rsa). RS needs root
  once, at install, and nothing at run time.
- **Use RL where a system unit cannot be installed but linger can be set.** Start the supervisor through the user manager,
  with `systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes` or a user unit. Treat linger as a precondition the engine checks at start:
  `loginctl show-user <user> -p Linger` reads `Linger=yes`.
- **Under either route, record each dispatch's cgroup path durably,** so that the next start can write `cgroup.kill` to an
  orphan. N3 showed that this works under both routes with no session open. It is the cgroup analogue of `R-STATE-10`.
- **Refuse R0 for unattended work.** A supervisor with neither linger nor a system unit has no writable cgroup when no
  session is open. If it started under a session, the user manager's stop ends its whole tree, supervisor included, about
  10 s after the last session closes (r0c). The engine should detect this case and refuse, rather than run without
  containment.
- **Under WSL2, state that a dispatch lives only as long as some `wsl.exe` client keeps the distro up.**

The next card must not assume these things:

- That a WSL login session closes when its command ends (s2).
- That the user manager's stop is harmless to a tree started under a session without linger. It sent `SIGTERM` and then
  `SIGKILL` to everything in the unit (r0c).
- That the unit's own cgroup is writable under RS (rsa).
- That every WSL login of a user opens a session. After a `terminate-session` in one distro instance, the next two logins
  of that user in that instance opened none (rlb, rsb; see "What failed").

**What would reverse the recommendation:**

- A native Linux host on which a system unit's `Delegate=yes` child cgroup is not writable by the unit's `User=`, or on
  which linger does not keep the user manager running with no session.
- A distribution or policy that stops lingering user managers, or kills units, at the last logout.
- A target that forbids both installing a system unit and setting linger. Rigger would then have no whole-tree
  containment with no session. Its fallback would be today's process-group route, with its gap at M6.
- Evidence that WSL2 keeps a distro up with no `wsl.exe` client. That would make WSL fit for long unattended runs, which
  this report finds it is not.

## What this would change, as a proposal

This section is a proposal for the PM, the architect and the owner. The pull request changes none of these.

| Document | What the recommendation would change |
|---|---|
| `D13` | It makes macOS v0's only host. A Linux host's containment backend would need a system unit or linger as an install precondition |
| `R-STATE-17` | It defines containment as a process group plus a census of the dispatch's directory. A delegated child cgroup per dispatch would also hold M6 |
| `R-STATE-9`, `R-STATE-10` and `R-STATE-11` | On the killed-outright path, the next start ends each recorded group. On Linux the recorded thing would be a cgroup path, which N3 showed a fresh process can reach with no session |
| `ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Failure model" | L0's containment paragraph, and what the engine checks at start on Linux: linger or a system unit |

## Custody

### The transcript

`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/transcript.txt` is the step runner's transcript, with an `ABOUTME` line added
above its first entry. It holds:

- every command run on the WSL2 machine and the Windows machine;
- each laptop step run through the runner: the final listing, the comparisons, the checks, the first commit and its push.

Each entry carries its exact argv, its stdin verbatim with a sha256, its UTC start and end, the host and the full
output. Inside a WSL step, each line the script printed carries its own UTC time.

`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/session-log.txt` holds every tool call in the spike engineer's session record,
with its UTC times, host, input and result: the laptop commands outside the runner, the writing of this report, and the
GitHub comments. It was generated by #1430's
`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/gen-session-log.py`, with only its input path, output path and header line
changed, as `docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/source/gen-session-log.py`.

| WSL2 | `/etc/systemd/system/fx1459-rs@.service`, then `systemctl daemon-reload` (rsa, rsc) | Yes. The file was removed, then `daemon-reload` and `reset-failed` ran |

The spike made no other change. It did not touch `[name withheld]` or its linger: s5 printed `Linger=yes` for `[name withheld]`, as found. It
installed no package, changed neither `.wslconfig` nor `/etc/wsl.conf`, ran no WSL restart or shutdown command, and
changed nothing on Windows.

**The Mac mini was not used.**

### The process record

`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/process-record.txt` lists every process that counts, one per line. Each entry
gives:

- the host;
- the pid;
- the creation time:
  - on WSL2, the boot id plus the `/proc` starttime, which is how the kernel reports it, with the UTC time derived from it
    beside them;
  - on Windows, `Win32_Process.CreationDate` in UTC;
- the UTC time the entry was recorded;
- the step in which it was recorded.

Each entry was written as the process started, by the process that started it, or by the step's own script before the
step returned. Either way it is earlier than the next command on that host.

- **The supervisor and each fresh writer** record themselves first.
- **The fixture** records each member as it starts it.
- **Each driver** records:
  - its own bash and its `/init` parents;
  - when its connection started the distro, every process of that start except `[name withheld]`'s;
  - every new process of the spike's own: the script's descendants, the user's processes, its units' processes, and
    root's session for the spike's own connection;
  - through interop, its connection's Windows chain and any WSL VM process started since that connection began.

The eight s1 entries for [name withheld]'s processes stay in the record, marked void by the dated note s2 wrote.

### Processes still running that the record does not hold

Each of the four hung attempts at the final Windows listing, f1, f1b, f1c and f1d, left its Windows session running
after `ssh` gave up. w8 found them afterwards, so each one is a disclosure, not a record. Each was still alive in f1f:

| Attempt | `sshd -R` and its creation time | `sshd -z` | cmd.exe | conhost.exe | powershell.exe |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| f1 | 14528, 2026-11-22T04:25:10.147Z | 16668 | 38256 | 36460 | 37980 |
| f1b | 752, 2026-11-22T04:27:16.129Z | 9284 | 12384 | 23476 | 33160 |
| f1c | 20460, 2026-11-22T04:29:38.018Z | 37344 | 3572 | 12604 | 12240 |
| f1d | 23332, 2026-11-22T04:31:47.610Z | 2500 | 37188 | 27504 | 37096 |

`AGENTS.md`, "End only recorded processes", lets a session end only processes whose pids it recorded at start. None of
these was, so the spike left them running. **They are for the owner to end.**

On the laptop, the harness's background shells were not recorded either: one Monitor loop that watched for the sibling
answers, and three polling loops. Each had exited before f3.

### After the last run, no recorded process is alive

| Host | Step | What it found |
|---|---|---|
| WSL2 | f2b, at 2026-11-22T04:39:25Z, in a distro instance started after rsc | It listed every process with its pid, `/proc` starttime and `ps` `lstart`, then checked each recorded WSL entry. It printed `checked 1022 recorded WSL entries against boot b7aea7c3-a116-46b4-9610-75a534a80142: 0 match on boot, pid and starttime` |
| Windows | f1f, at 2026-11-22T04:41:17Z, after the WSL VM had stopped (no `vmmemWSL` is listed) | It listed every process with its pid and creation time. c2 compared that listing with the record and printed `windows: 334 processes listed, 194 record entries, 0 match on both pid and creation time []` |
| Laptop | f3 | `ps -axo pid=,lstart=,comm=` listed every process with its pid and start time. The record holds no laptop entry, because every command the spike ran on the laptop exited before it returned, apart from the background shells above, so nothing listed can match one |

The record does not hold f2b's and f1f's own processes, since a listing cannot list itself as ended.

## Incidental findings

- **[name withheld] holds a `[name withheld]` login session in WSL whenever WSL runs.** That is a fact about the owner's own tool, recorded at
  [1459#7106919811](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1459#issuecomment-7106919811), not a defect in Rigger. Any
  later spike on this host that needs a user with no session needs its own user, as this one did.
- **The hung Windows SSH sessions, f1 to f1d, were not root-caused.** They are a defect in this spike's throwaway harness,
  not in Rigger, so they get no card. Their leftovers are disclosed above.

### 132. 2026-11-22T04:48:26.729Z -> 2026-11-22T04:48:26.922Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Snapshot transcript and failed runs into evidence dir"}

# ABOUTME: Card #1459: copies the step transcript into the evidence directory with an ABOUTME line, removing the NUL bytes
# that wsl.exe's UTF-16 output left (the header states their count and the original's sha256), and writes each failed run's entry.
import hashlib, re, sys, os
E = sys.argv[1]
raw = open('transcript.txt', 'rb').read()
sha = hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest(); nul = raw.count(b'\x00')
body = raw.replace(b'\x00', b'').decode('utf-8')
head = (f'ABOUTME: Card #1459 step-runner transcript: every step\'s exact argv, stdin script, UTC times, host, exit and full output. '
        f'This copy removes {nul} NUL bytes, the UTF-16 padding in wsl.exe -l -v output; nothing else differs. Original sha256 {sha}, {len(raw)} bytes.\n')
open(os.path.join(E, 'transcript.txt'), 'w').write(head + body)
os.[name withheld](os.path.join(E, 'failed-runs'), exist_ok=True)
for step in sys.argv[2:]:
    m = re.search(r'^### step ' + re.escape(step) + r' .*?(?=^### step |\Z)', body, re.M | re.S)
    open(os.path.join(E, 'failed-runs', step + '.txt'), 'w').write(
        f'ABOUTME: Card #1459 failed run {step}: its transcript entry, copied whole from transcript.txt beside this directory.\n' + m.group(0))
print(sha, nul)
PY

WSL entries: boot= is the VM's boot id and starttime= is field 22 of /proc/<pid>/stat (clock ticks since that boot); together they are the
creation time as the kernel reports it, and created= is the UTC time derived from them (supervisor, writer and member lines omit created=).
Windows entries: created= is Win32_Process.CreationDate in UTC. recorded= is when the entry was written. Member lines were written into the
run's members.txt by the process that started the member, as it started it; their boot id is the run's supervisor's.
VOID (note written 2026-11-22 by step s2 into the WSL record file, and kept here): the eight step=s1 lines labelled "end:" for pids 649, 677,
678, 686, 693, 701, 702 and 706 are [name withheld]'s SSH login as [name withheld] and [name withheld]'s user manager, not the spike's. s1's scan recorded every new process
in user.slice; the scan was narrowed before s2. They are kept, not deleted, and are no part of the spike's record.
Not in this record (see the report, "Processes still running that the record does not hold"): the Windows sessions the hung steps f1, f1b,
f1c and f1d left running, the laptop's background shells, step p0b's Windows processes (it started the WSL VM before any recording
existed), and the final listings' own processes.
