### 136. 2026-11-22T04:49:06.972Z -> 2026-11-22T04:49:07.371Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write journal entry and register report in doc-references"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer && cat > docs/journal/2026-11-22-0450-1459-whole-tree-without-a-session.md <<'[name withheld]'
ABOUTME: Journal for #1459: whether an unprivileged Linux supervisor under WSL2 ends an agent's whole tree with cgroup.kill
when its user has no login session.

# #1459: Whole-tree containment with no login session

## What ran

On 2026-11-22, #1430's fixture ran under WSL2 for a dedicated user created for the card, `rigger-spike`. It ran under
three routes: R0 (no linger), RL (linger set) and RS (a system unit). It ran three cases: no session at start (N1), the
last session closing while the tree runs (N2), and the supervisor killed outright with no session, followed by a fresh
writer (N3). The code stayed in the gitignored `spikes/`. Its source, every listing, the transcript and the process
record are in [the report](../spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session.md).

## What we learned

- **No linger and no system unit means no containment once the user logs out.** With no session, the supervisor sits in a
  root-owned cgroup. If it started under a session, the user manager's stop kills the whole tree about 10 s after the
  last session closes. That is systemd acting, not Rigger.
- **Linger or a system unit makes `cgroup.kill` work with no session,** in N1, N2 and N3 alike, in under 2 ms. Under a
  system unit, only the child cgroup the supervisor makes is the user's. The unit's own `cgroup.kill` stays root's.
- **WSL2 stops the distro 10 to 20 s after its last `wsl.exe` client exits,** whatever runs inside it. An SSH login into
  WSL does not keep it up, and neither does linger.
- **A WSL login session outlives the command that opened it.** It stays open until something terminates it, or until
  the distro stops.
- **A host the owner uses is not empty.** [name withheld] logs into WSL as `[name withheld]` at every distro start. A spike that needs a
  user with no session needs a user of its own, and that needs the owner's ruling first.

## What failed

- **An interop `powershell.exe` ate the rest of a script fed to `bash -s`.** Every script now runs its body as a function
  with stdin from `/dev/null`.
- **After one `terminate-session`, WSL opened no new login session for that user in the same distro instance.** Two N2
  runs therefore did not count, and were rerun in fresh instances. That meant deleting the user too early, then
  recreating it.
- **A process scan by cgroup recorded someone else's processes.** Scanning by the spike's own tree fixed it, and the bad
  lines were voided with a note rather than deleted.
- **Four Windows listings that fed PowerShell a long stdin hung** and left their sessions running. Those sessions were
  not recorded at start, so they went to the owner instead of being ended.
[name withheld]

### 168. 2026-11-22T05:15:22.391Z -> 2026-11-22T05:15:24.769Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Open the docs-only pull request"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer && cat > spikes/1459-spike-engineer/pr.md <<'[name withheld]'
Closes #1459

Docs-only spike report: whether an unprivileged Linux supervisor under WSL2 gets a delegated cgroup and ends an agent's whole tree with cgroup.kill when its user has no login session. The user under test is `rigger-spike`, a dedicated WSL user created under the owner's ruling (1459#7106921307) and deleted at the end, with proof.

- **R0** (no linger, nothing changed): not obtained with no session open. A logout while a tree runs lets the user manager kill the whole tree about 10 s later, before anything can write cgroup.kill.
- **RL** (linger) and **RS** (a system unit with `Delegate=yes`): cgroup.kill ended every member in N1, N2 and N3, in 0.27 to 1.27 ms. Under RS, only the child cgroup the supervisor makes is writable; the unit's own cgroup.kill stays root's.
- **WSL bounds every route.** The distro stops 10 to 20 s after its last `wsl.exe` client exits.

Lands in:

- the report: `docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session.md`;
- its evidence: `docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/`, with the transcript, session log, process record, failed runs and source;
- the journal entry: `docs/journal/2026-11-22-0450-1459-whole-tree-without-a-session.md`;
- the report's entry in `doc-references.json`.

The throwaway code stays in the gitignored spikes directory. No requirement, decision or `ARCHITECTURE.md` text changes; the report's proposals are for the PM, the architect and the owner.

**For the owner:** four hung SSH sessions on the Windows machine, left by steps f1 to f1d, are still running. None of them was recorded at start, so the spike did not end them. Their pids are in the report, under "Processes still running that the record does not hold".

This binds to acceptance 25 and “Every pid, recorded at its start.” To become sound, supply genuine contemporaneous records for all covered processes where recoverable, and replace unsupported experiment evidence with correctly recorded runs where needed. Do not turn a later discovery into an at-start record. Any irrecoverable historical exception belongs to the owner, not the maker or judge.

**F2 — Repeated run names overwrite boot provenance (item 25).** source/gen-record.py lines 6–10 stores only the last supervisor boot per run name; line 27 uses it for every historical member dump. process-record.txt line 1005 assigns rl-n2 root pid 3062/starttime 7976, recorded at 04:21:17.512492Z, boot `b7aea7c3-a116-46b4-9610-75a534a80142`. Its actual supervisor in rlb (record line 819 and transcript line 4520) has boot `3c4522f0-1741-42f8-9d01-88ef813a8399`. The member's original timestamp is preserved at transcript line 6183. This defect is introduced by this PR and undermines identity-based custody checks.

```sh

This binds to acceptance 25's host-reported creation identity.

[earlier head] Bring #1459's transcript and session log up to the first push
# ABOUTME: Card #1459: builds the process record from the step transcript: every REC line (WSL and Windows) and every fixture MEMBER
# line, with the step it came from. A MEMBER line takes its boot id from the latest supervisor record for its run name before the
# step that printed it, so a run name used twice (rl-n2 in rlb and rlc, rs-n2 in rsb and rsc) keeps each execution's own boot.
import re, sys, collections
t = open('transcript.txt', encoding='utf-8', errors='replace').read().split('\n')
step = None; out = []; seen = set(); runboot = {}
cur_run = None
for l in t:
    m = re.match(r'### step (\S+)', l)
    if m: step = m.group(1); continue
    m = re.match(r'=== FILE \S+/runs/([^/]+)/members.txt', l)
    if m: cur_run = m.group(1); continue
    if l.startswith('=== END'): cur_run = None; continue
    m = re.match(r'REC host=wsl boot=(\S+) pid=(\d+) starttime=(\d+).*label=supervisor:(\S+)', l)
    if m: runboot[m.group(4)] = m.group(1)
    if l.startswith('REC host='):
        k = re.sub(r' recorded=\S+', '', l)
        if k in seen: continue
        seen.add(k); out.append(f'step={step} {l}')
    m = re.match(r'MEMBER (\S+) (\d+) (\d+) (\S+)$', l)
    if m and cur_run:
        k = ('M', cur_run, m.group(2), m.group(3))
        if k in seen: continue
        seen.add(k)
        out.append(f'step={step} REC host=wsl boot={runboot.get(cur_run, "?")} pid={m.group(2)} starttime={m.group(3)} recorded={m.group(4)} label=member:{cur_run}:{m.group(1)} (written by the process that started it, into the run\'s members.txt)')
open(sys.argv[1], 'w').write('\n'.join(out) + '\n')
c = collections.Counter(re.search(r'host=(\w+)', x).group(1) for x in out)
print(len(out), dict(c))

### 176. 2026-11-22T23:32:45.981Z -> 2026-11-22T23:32:46.354Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Check member boot provenance in corrected and old record"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer && cat > check-boots.py <<'PY'
# ABOUTME: Card #1459: checks that every fixture member in the process record carries the boot id of the supervisor execution
# that started it, taken as the latest supervisor of the same run name recorded before the member.
import re, sys
sups = []; bad = 0; n = 0
for l in open(sys.argv[1]):
    m = re.search(r'boot=(\S+) pid=\d+ starttime=\d+ recorded=(\S+) label=supervisor:(\S+)', l)
    if m: sups.append((m.group(2), m.group(3), m.group(1)))
for l in open(sys.argv[1]):
    m = re.search(r'boot=(\S+) pid=(\d+) starttime=\d+ recorded=(\S+) label=member:([^:]+):', l)
    if not m: continue
    n += 1
    cands = [s for s in sups if s[1] == m.group(4) and s[0] <= m.group(3)]
    want = max(cands)[2] if cands else None
    if want != m.group(1): bad += 1; print('MISMATCH', l.strip()[:160], 'supervisor boot', want)
print(f'{n} member entries checked, {bad} carry a boot other than their own supervisor execution\'s')
PY
node orch.cjs c3 laptop - -- python3 check-boots.py record-fixed.txt; node orch.cjs c3old laptop - -- python3 check-boots.py ../../docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/process-record.txt | tail -1
--- result ---
72 member entries checked, 0 carry a boot other than their own supervisor execution's
72 member entries checked, 16 carry a boot other than their own supervisor execution's

# ABOUTME: Card #1459: compares a final process listing with the process record's entries for one host, matching on the host's
# creation identity: boot id, pid and /proc starttime on WSL; pid and creation time on Windows; pid and start time on the laptop.
import re, sys
host, rec_file, listing = sys.argv[1:4]
L = open(listing, encoding='utf-8', errors='replace').read().split('\n')
if host == 'wsl':
    boot = re.search(r'boot_id (\S+)', L[0]).group(1)
    rec = {(m.group(1), m.group(2), m.group(3)) for l in open(rec_file) for m in [re.search(r'host=wsl boot=(\S+) pid=(\d+) starttime=(\d+)', l)] if m}
    lst = [(boot,) + tuple(l.split()[:2]) for l in L if re.match(r'^\d+ \d+ ', l)]
elif host == 'windows':
    rec = {(m.group(1), m.group(2)) for l in open(rec_file) for m in [re.search(r'host=windows pid=(\d+) ppid=\d+ created=(\S+)', l)] if m}
    lst = [tuple(l.split()[:2]) for l in L if re.match(r'^\d+ \S+ ', l)]
else:
    rec = {(m.group(1), m.group(2)) for l in open(rec_file) for m in [re.search(r'host=laptop pid=(\d+) created=(.+?) label=', l)] if m}
    lst = [(l.split()[0], ' '.join(l.split()[1:6])) for l in L if re.match(r'^ *\d+ ', l)]
hits = [x for x in lst if x in rec]
print(f'{host}: {len(lst)} processes listed, {len(rec)} record entries, {len(hits)} match on the creation identity', hits)
PY

### 179. 2026-11-22T23:33:33.103Z -> 2026-11-22T23:33:33.324Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Generate exact list of unrecorded processes"}
cd /Users/[name withheld]/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1459-spike-engineer/spikes/1459-spike-engineer && cat > gen-unrecorded.py <<'PY'
# ABOUTME: Card #1459: lists, from the transcript's own listings, every process the spike knows it started but did not record at
# its start, with pid and creation time where a listing printed one. Output lines start UNRECORDED and name the listing they come from.
import re, sys
T = open('transcript.txt', encoding='utf-8', errors='replace').read().replace('\x00', '')
def out(step):
    m = re.search(r'^### step ' + step + r' .*?(?=^### step |\Z)', T, re.M | re.S).group(0)
    return m.split('--- output ---\n', 1)[1].split('\n')
L = []
def ps_rows(step, why):
    o = out(step); i = next(k for k, l in enumerate(o) if l.startswith('--- ps -eo'))
    for l in o[i + 2:]:
        m = re.match(r'^\s*(\d+)\s+(\d+)\s+(\S+)\s+(\w{3} \w{3} +\d+ [\d:]+ \d{4})\s+(\S+)\s+(.*)$', l)
        if not m: break
        pid, ppid, user, lstart, cg, args = m.groups()
        if user == '[name withheld]': continue
        if pid in ('577', '578') and step == 'p0b': pass
        L.append(f'UNRECORDED step={step} host=wsl pid={pid} ppid={ppid} user={user} created="{lstart}" (ps lstart, UTC, whole seconds) listed-in={step} why={why} args={args[:90]}')
ps_rows('p0b', 'probe-before-recording-existed;its-connection-started-the-WSL-distro')
ps_rows('p1', 'probe-before-recording-existed;its-connection-started-the-WSL-distro;[name withheld]-lines-were-filtered-out-of-this-listing')
for l in out('p1'):
    m = re.match(r'^\s*(\d+)\s+(\d+) (2026-11-22T02:5\S+) (vmwp\.exe|vmmemWSL|wslrelay\.exe|wslhost\.exe|conhost\.exe)\s*(.*)$', l)
    if m and m.group(3) >= '2026-11-22T02:59:21':
        L.append(f'UNRECORDED step=p1 host=windows pid={m.group(1)} ppid={m.group(2)} created={m.group(3)} listed-in=p1 why=probe-before-recording-existed;VM-or-WSL-host-process-that-could-outlive-the-command name={m.group(4)} {m.group(5)[:80]}')
for l in out('p2'):
    m = re.match(r'^\s*(\d+)\s+(\d+)\s+(root)\s+(\w{3} \w{3} +\d+ [\d:]+ \d{4})\s+(\S+)\s+(sshd: /usr/sbin/sshd.*)$', l)
    if m: L.append(f'UNRECORDED step=p2 host=wsl pid={m.group(1)} ppid={m.group(2)} user=root created="{m.group(4)}" listed-in=p2 why=probe-before-recording-existed;its-connection-started-the-WSL-distro args={m.group(6)[:90]}')
for l in out('w8'):

ABOUTME: Card #1459 process record: every process that counts, one per line, with host, pid, creation time and the UTC time it was recorded.
Generated by source/gen-record.py from transcript.txt beside this file. Each line starts with the step whose transcript entry printed it.
WSL entries: boot= is the VM's boot id and starttime= is field 22 of /proc/<pid>/stat (clock ticks since that boot); together they are the
creation time as the kernel reports it, and created= is the UTC time derived from them (supervisor, writer and member lines omit created=).
Windows entries: created= is Win32_Process.CreationDate in UTC. recorded= is when the entry was written. Member lines were written into the
run's members.txt by the process that started the member, as it started it. A member's boot id is that of the supervisor execution that
started it: the latest supervisor record of the same run name before the member, so rl-n2 and rs-n2 keep rlb's and rsb's boot apart from
rlc's and rsc's (round 1 of this record wrongly gave all sixteen of rlb's and rsb's member lines the later boot; step c3 checks the fix).
VOID (note written 2026-11-22 by step s2 into the WSL record file, and kept here): the eight step=s1 lines labelled "end:" for pids 649, 677,
678, 686, 693, 701, 702 and 706 are [name withheld]'s SSH login as [name withheld] and [name withheld]'s user manager, not the spike's. s1's scan recorded every new process
in user.slice; the scan was narrowed before s2. They are kept, not deleted, and are no part of the spike's record.
The REC lines are the record. The UNRECORDED lines at the end are not: they are the processes the spike started that count under the card's
"Every pid, recorded at its start" but were not recorded at their start. That was required and the spike failed to do it; the gap cannot be
recovered. Each line gives the pid and creation time where a listing printed them, and why it was not recorded.

### Processes the spike started but did not record at their start

**This is a defect in the spike, and it cannot be recovered.** The card requires every process that counts to be recorded
as it starts. The processes below count under that definition, and none was recorded at its start. A pid found
afterwards is a disclosure, not a record, so they stay out of the record's REC lines. The owner's standing rule for the
Temporal track's spikes ([1453#7087640218](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1453#issuecomment-7087640218))
accepts an unrecoverable gap when the report lists it exactly and admits the defect. This is that list.

`docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/source/gen-unrecorded.py` generated these lines from the transcript's own
listings. The same lines close `docs/spikes/whole-tree-without-a-session/process-record.txt`, under an `UNRECORDED`
heading.

**The four hung listing sessions on Windows (f1, f1b, f1c, f1d).** When `ssh` gave up on each, its Windows session went
on running. w8 found the 20 processes afterwards, with the creation times below. rigger-ae later ended all 20 on the
owner's authorization. It first matched each `sshd`'s creation time to this report, and a follow-up listing found none
alive ([1459#7085291842](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1459#issuecomment-7085291842)).

**The three read-only probes, p0b, p1 and p2, which ran before any recording existed.**

- p0b's and p1's connections each started the WSL distro, and each also started the VM.
- Their WSL processes, other than `[name withheld]`'s, are listed below with the creation time each probe's `ps` printed, in whole
  seconds, in UTC. That covers the distro's start, the probe's own chain, root's login session and root's user manager.
- p1's listing filtered out [name withheld], so those pids are unknown.
- p1's Windows VM processes are listed with their creation times. p0b printed no Windows listing, so its VM's pids are
  unknown.
- p2's connection restarted the distro, but p2 printed only `[name withheld]`'s and `sshd`'s processes. Of its processes, only its
  `sshd` listener is known.
- The probes' own Windows connection chains (`sshd`, cmd.exe, conhost.exe and wsl.exe) are unknown. Every later WSL step
  recorded its own.

**The laptop.** The step runner prints its pid only when a step ends. Some steps ran from a shell the harness had put in
the background. For those, the shell, the runner and the runner's child were still running when the Bash call returned.
Their pids are known from each step's header, but their creation times are not. That covers w9-1, w10-1, f2b, f1f, c2
and the commits and pushes k1, k2, k4 and k5. For k1, k2 and k4 it also covers every process the commit hook's test
suite started. The harness's Monitor loop, which watched for the sibling answers, never printed its pid. f4, a laptop
listing taken after k5, shows none of the known laptop pids alive.
